## 2026-11-08 — engagement versus route, third reading: which class do CO's own words favour? One exclusion; the class open

**The question as left.** C8 lets a commitment engage a quantum in transit through a channel. Which contacts through a channel are engagements (commitments, irreversible by primitive) rather than routes (coherent)? Three classes were named: a rate law with amplification by number (GRW-type), a threshold law (Penrose-type), an emergent record (decoherence).

**The emergent class is excluded for CO by its premise, not by experiment.** CO takes "things happen" as primitive: a commitment is an event, definite, irreversible, and time is their count. If definiteness were emergent (a description of a decohered branch), the Born identification would lose its referent, time as a count would have nothing to count, and the Bell account would become branching with no local event. Excluding it is the author's premise; an ontology that started elsewhere could keep it. Recorded as the premise doing the work, not as a result.

**The simplest reading of C8 is excluded by experiment.** Read literally, C8 makes every absorption through an open channel a commitment, at a chance per contact equal to the channel's coupling. Cavity QED shows absorption through an open channel that is coherent and reversible: a single atom and a single photon in a high-Q cavity exchange the quantum back and forth (vacuum Rabi oscillation in time, Brune et al. 1996; the corresponding normal-mode splitting in an optical cavity, Thompson–Rempe–Kimble 1992), the exchange staying coherent over a Rabi period of ~10⁻⁵ s (microwave) to ~10⁻⁷ s (optical), some 10²⁰–10²¹ of the atom's own renewals (ω ≈ 10²⁶ s⁻¹), with no event at any rate comparable to the coupling. Resonant Rabi flopping in a laser or microwave field (Ramsey, NMR, atomic clocks) does the same in free space. So "through a channel" is necessary and not sufficient, and the chance per contact is not the channel's coupling as such. A small chance per passage (ε ≪ 1 per Rabi period) is bounded, not excluded. The coupling yields a rate only where the final states form a continuum (golden rule), and a continuum is not a commitment either: decay into it is unitary. The C8 wording ("the chance per contact that a channel engages is the channel's coupling, imported") is corrected below to say the coupling sets how often the channel is *used*, not how often its use is a commitment.

**What separates the two in the cases cited.** Coherent passage through a channel (vacuum Rabi oscillation, resonant Rabi flopping, the coherent part of resonance fluorescence, nuclear forward scattering) leaves the engager able to re-emit the quantum coherently, with no further pattern's state changed. Refraction, Rayleigh and Raman are off-resonant and do not pass the channel test (11-07); they are not cases of it. Irreversible absorption (photodetection, a ³He tube, a silver-halide grain) passes the quantum on: the engager's new state engages further patterns (a photoelectron is multiplied at a dynode or in an avalanche, or drives a current through an amplifier, a proton and triton deposit 764 keV in the gas, a grain's electron reduces silver), so the quantum's frequency is spread over many patterns' rates. Standard physics calls this amplification and treats it as where decoherence is fast. In CO's words: *an engagement is a commitment when the engaged quantum is itself engaged onward before it can be returned.* As stated, this is decoherence's own criterion (amplification; redundancy in quantum Darwinism). It becomes a posit of CO's only with a primitive stopping number, which puts it in the threshold family, not the rate family.

**Why it is a shape and not a candidate.** "Before it can be returned" needs a time, and the rules give a pattern no clock for "could still return." "Engaged onward" moves the question one step down the chain: the onward engagement is a commitment by the same criterion, so the definition is recursive and needs a stopping rule (a count of onward engagements, which is a threshold with a number the rules do not supply, or "until it reaches a pattern that renews many times per return time," which reintroduces a ratio of rates as the threshold). Each stopping rule is the threshold class with a constant; none is in the table. The recursion goes if passages are counted rather than engagements: a passage through a channel is a commitment when the chain of passages it sets off reaches N. With the stopping rule "until recombination is infeasible" this is the emergent class, excluded above by premise; so the premise requires N to be physical, and a physical N predicts that reversal fails beyond it. Multiple-quantum NMR echoes (double-quantum Hamiltonian, not flip-flops) reverse correlations spread through thousands of spins, and polarisation echoes reverse a quantum passed by flip-flops through ~10–10² (Baum–Munowitz–Garroway–Pines 1985; Krojanski–Suter 2004; ion chains of about 100, Gärttner 2017; counts from memory, to be checked): N is at least of that order if each flip-flop counts, and the unnumbered shape ("engaged onward before it can be returned") is excluded. The ratio-of-rates stopping rule is excluded by the Rabi atom itself, which renews about 10²¹ times per return time. Quantum erasers and weak-measurement reversal act only before amplification and do not bear on it. A photodetector's chain reaches ~10⁶–10⁹ passages within nanoseconds, so N has a window. So the reading locates the posit (a condition on onward engagement) and prices it (one stopping rule, one number), and stops there.

**Resolution through correlation, not ruled.** QND photon counting (Guerlin et al. 2007) resolves photon number with atoms that cross the cavity off-resonance and are detected afterwards: the photon is never engaged through a channel, yet its pattern is resolved. Ion fluorescence readout is the same case. C8 resolves only "the pattern whose transit it was"; how engaging one pattern resolves another correlated with it (presumably through the configuration amplitude) is owed. Thermal emission and collisional decoherence (Hackermüller 2004; Hornberger 2003) remove fringes with no channel absorption at all: entanglement without a commitment, consistent, stated.

**Grade.** Emergent class excluded by premise (the author's). "Every channel absorption is a commitment" excluded by cavity QED at the coupling's strength; a small residual bounded. The posit owed: shape, a chain of passages with a primitive stopping number N, bounded below by echo experiments (thousands of passages) and above by detector chains (~10⁶–10⁹); without N it is the emergent class. Resolution through correlation not ruled. C8's wording amended in `MATTER.md` and the plain doc (cold reading of the same day applied).

## 2026-11-08 — where the stopping number N could sit (reading; shape)

**The shape as left.** A passage through a channel is a commitment when the chain of passages it sets off reaches N; with N physical, reversal fails beyond it; without N, the emergent class the premise excludes.

**Lower bound (citations checked; counts and conversion from the papers' fits).** Solid-state multiple-quantum NMR drives clusters of correlated proton spins forward under the dipolar (double-quantum) Hamiltonian and then reverses it; the cluster size is read only through the reversal, so a measured size is a size that was reversed. Krojanski–Suter 2004 (PRL 93, 090501): up to ~650 spins; Krojanski–Suter 2006 (PRA 74, 062319): "several 1000"; Álvarez–Suter–Kaiser 2015 (Science 349, 846): up to ~7000. The reversal is imperfect and its loss grows smoothly with size (decoherence rate ∝ K^{~1/2}), with no step. Trapped ions: >100 (Gärttner et al. 2017). So, if each correlated spin costs at least one passage (K spins need at least K−1 two-body steps), N ≳ 7×10³ in operator-support size, with K model-dependent to about a factor of 2 (a Gaussian fit to the coherence-order distribution); no sharp failure has been seen below it. The reversal is partial; the bound holds because a hard N would remove signal from every seed. Caveat: the double-quantum Hamiltonian flips spin pairs with quanta absorbed from a classical rf drive, and the seed is a term of a mixed state, not a quantum in transit. The chain is not set off by a quantum through a channel unless driven passages count. If they do not, the bound falls to flip-flop polarisation echoes, about 10–10² spins. If they do, driven amplify-and-reverse experiments (parametric amplifiers, spinor-BEC and cavity-twisting echoes, up to ~10⁵ atoms) count too.

**Upper window.** Every detector's chain passes 10⁴ long before anyone reads it. This bounds N only if the click is already definite at that stage, which is what is at issue; the firm upper bound is the chain size by the time a record is read (macroscopic). For scale: a photomultiplier's gain is ~10⁶–10⁷ electrons; a single rhodopsin activation hydrolyses ~10⁵ cGMP; a photographic latent image is only a few silver atoms, and is not yet permanent (it can fade) until development multiplies it ~10⁹-fold. So 7×10³ ≲ N (driven passages counted; ~10² if not), and N is below a macroscopic record. The detector stages (10⁵–10⁹) mark where amplification is fast, not a bound. The latent image is not a case: it needs several photons, one photon's chain in the grain is many passages, and fading is chemical, not coherent return. Passages are counted between patterns, a collective mode counting as one; counted per atom moved, single-phonon swaps into ~10¹³–10¹⁷-atom resonators (O'Connell 2010; Bild 2023) would exclude the window.

**Does any number in CO land there?** CO's constants are δ ≈ 10⁻³² (equivalently the hop length ℓ ≈ 10⁻¹⁸ m), P_max ≈ 3ħ and Λ. None gives 10⁴–10⁹ by any combination that is not numerology. So N would be **a new constant**, CO's fourth, entered for measurement as δ was entered for gravity.

**What it would predict.** A truncation of reversed chain sizes at N, sharp in size, with a floor that better isolation does not lower. Perturbation-independent echo decay is already reported in NMR and explained by standard quantum mechanics (complexity amplifies residual error), so the mark is the size cutoff, not isolation-independence. Standard quantum mechanics and decoherence predict a smooth loss that improves with better isolation and has no floor in principle. The NMR data so far are smooth up to ~7000. A clean test: push reversed cluster sizes upward with improving control and look for a size-independent ceiling. This is the first place a difference from quantum mechanics could appear, if the shape is adopted, driven passages are counted, and N is physical; what is specific to CO, against other threshold models, is only that the threshold is a count.

**Grade.** Shape, with its window bounded by experiment (lower bound conditional on counting driven passages; upper bound macroscopic, detector stages not a bound). Two clauses owed before any ruling: whether driven passages count; that a collective mode is one pattern. Not a candidate until ruled whether measurement gets a fourth constant.

## 2026-11-08 — resolution through correlation: QND photon counting read against C8, the Bell candidate and the branched-field ruling (candidate reading); and what it says about driven passages

**The case.** Haroche's group counts photons in a cavity without absorbing them (Guerlin et al. 2007; Gleyzes et al. 2007): Rydberg atoms cross the cavity off-resonance, acquire a phase that depends on the photon number, and are then field-ionised and counted. The photon number is resolved progressively over tens of atoms, each atom's outcome only reweighting the photon-number distribution, though no photon is ever engaged through a channel. C8 as written resolves only "the pattern whose transit it was."

**Reading.** The rules already on the board cover it in three steps.
1. *The atom and the field become one stock.* With the branched-field ruling (candidate), two patterns coupled through a field have one amplitude over their joint configuration; interaction makes them one stock (interaction-made entanglement, entered as a consequence of that ruling). The dispersive crossing is a route: no event, coherent, and afterwards the atom's phase and the photon number are one configuration amplitude.
2. *C8 engages the atom.* Field ionisation is state-selective tunnelling in a quasi-static field, not a passage through a channel (a static field has no frequency to match); the first channel passage is the freed electron striking the multiplier. C8 as written resolves the engaged pattern; that the engager's own prior state (the atom's level) becomes definite is a further reading, owed.
3. *One stock, cut once.* The Bell candidate already says a stock shared across patterns is cut once by a commitment that engages any of it, each later commitment finding it as it stands in its own past cone. A commitment fixes the atom's outcome; the shared amplitude is conditioned on it (the field's amplitudes reweighted by the probability of that outcome given each photon number: the Lüders update, imported with the joint law, not in the rules). The photon number becomes definite only as successive atoms' conditionings concentrate it.

So C8's "the pattern whose transit it was is resolved" reads as: the engaged pattern is resolved, and whatever shares a joint amplitude with it is conditioned on the outcome, not resolved. Cost: a joint amplitude has no boundary in practice, so this is an update of one global amplitude, and it puts more weight on the no-signalling cost already open (dent intensity |a|² at far places now depends on local outcomes). ("Stock" is kept for the free stock; a pattern's or pair's possibility is called its amplitude here.) This is the Bell candidate applied, not a new rule: an entangled pair and a probe correlated with a system are the same object. Costs inherited, not added: the joint law is standard quantum mechanics'; where the resolved stock's possibility at other places goes is still not ruled.

**What it says about driven passages: less than it seems.** Detector chains are driven (field ionisation, multiplier and bias voltages), but if driven passages did not count, the commitment would still occur downstream in undriven dissipation (load resistor, electronics, the photocathode's hot-electron cascade), so detectors do not force the question. A line can also be drawn: the drive bends routes between passages (photomultiplier, avalanche photodiode, gas counter: each impact is a two-pattern passage, the striking electron handing on the quantum; passages count) versus the drive is a partner in each passage, supplying the flipped quanta (double-quantum NMR; would not count). Under that line the lower bound is ~10–10² (flip-flop polarisation echoes); laser-driven trapped-ion clusters need the same check. Whether driven passages count stays open. A second choice comes with it: count *quanta handed on*, not *patterns moved*. A single-phonon swap into a resonator is then one passage, which removes the resonator exclusion without a collective-mode clause (and the clause as drawn was not principled: BEC and twisted-ensemble atoms are distinct patterns, as NMR spins are, and MATTER.md has no rule for condensation). Under quanta counting, amplifier-type driven reversals (spin-mixing; parametric gain, SU(1,1)) move many quanta and would raise the bound if driven passages count; a pure twisting echo moves none.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected before push.** "One stock, cut once" was wrong for progressive measurement (each atom reweights; no single cut resolves n); field ionisation is not a channel passage; widening C8 to "stock" would either make every engagement fix the world or reduce to a global update, the latter named as a cost; the driven-passages argument was invalid (commitment would occur downstream anyway, and a principled line exists); the collective-mode clause was not principled. Lüders conditioning is imported with the joint law, owed as the ruling.

**Grade.** Candidate reading: QND as repeated conditioning of a joint amplitude, the conditioning rule imported (owed: the author's word on importing it). "Driven passages count": open, two lines stated. What a passage counts (quanta handed on vs patterns moved): open, quanta counting preferred on the resonator evidence. "A collective mode is one pattern": shape, not principled as stated.

## 2026-11-08 — three rulings on the measurement shape (the author's, candidate)

1. *The conditioning (Lüders) update is imported with the joint law:* a commitment fixes the engaged pattern's outcome; what shares a joint amplitude with it is conditioned on that outcome. The same import CO already makes for Bell, one step later.
2. *Passages are counted as quanta handed on, not patterns moved.* A single-phonon swap into a 10¹³–10¹⁷-atom resonator is one passage; the collective-mode clause is withdrawn as unneeded.
3. *A drive counts when it only steers between passages, not when it is a partner in each passage.* Photomultiplier, avalanche and gas-counter chains count; double-quantum NMR does not. Lower bound on N: ~10–10² (flip-flop polarisation echoes); amplifier-type driven reversals (spin-mixing, parametric gain) to be checked under this line.

## 2026-11-08 — the stopping number from the web's spare capacity (the author's idea; shape, with its natural definitions excluded)

**The idea, tightened.** A superposed body is a dense web of patterns and relations; the relations can carry only so much before they saturate; how near a web is to saturation sets where a chain of passages through it becomes a commitment. So N is not one universal number but follows from the web's structure: diamond and coal, the same carbon, would have different tipping points even perfectly isolated. If it can be made to work, N follows from the rules (P_max is already the table's saturation point) instead of being a fourth constant.

**Standard physics and present data.** In quantum mechanics a perfectly isolated body never decoheres, whatever its structure; diamond's long spin coherence is environmental: a quiet environment (isotopically purified ¹²C, spin 0; few paramagnetic impurities; a stiff lattice). For a composite body, the centre of mass can still lose coherence to its own internal states (gravitational time-dilation decoherence, Pikovski et al. 2015). The idea therefore makes a different claim, and it is bounded: structurally very different molecules (fullerenes; flexible organics up to ~25 kDa, ~2000 atoms) interfere as quantum mechanics predicts when cold and isolated, with visibilities matching it within errors. This bounds a structure-dependent N only if passages inside the molecule count and a commitment inside it resolves the centre of mass; neither is ruled. Levitated-nanoparticle interferometry (10⁶–10⁹ atoms, planned) is where a structure-dependent threshold would show.

**Defining "spare capacity" in the table's words.** Three readings, each tried against one test: detectors commit, and a detector's chain (10⁶–10²⁰ passages by the time a record is read) is a tiny part of the detector.
1. *Free stock of the web's support.* F = Σ P_free over the places the body spans, N = F/ħ. With P_free ~ P_max ≈ 3ħ and ℓ ≈ 10⁻¹⁸ m, a C₇₀ molecule spans ~5×10²⁶ places (F/ħ ~ 10²⁷), a 100 nm sphere ~10³³, 1 mm³ of diamond ~10⁴⁵. A detector's chain never reaches its own F/ħ: no detector would commit. **Excluded.** A local version fails the same way: each atom the chain touches spans ~10²⁴ places.
2. *A fraction of the web's relations (bonds).* N = f × (relations in the web); structure enters through bonds per atom (σ bonds: diamond 2 per atom, graphite 1.5; by bond order both are 2; coal is not graphite), which is the diamond/coal difference in its simplest form. The same test fails: a photomultiplier's 10⁶–10⁷ electrons, or even a read-out circuit's ~10²⁰ passages, are a negligible fraction of the ~10²³–10²⁵ bonds in the device unless f ≲ 10⁻⁵ against a macroscopic record (≲10²⁰ passages, a thermal record; detector stages are not a bound), at which point f is itself a new constant and the structure dependence is a factor of order one on top of it. **Excluded as a reason; survives only as a constant times a structure factor.**
3. *The web the chain itself has reached.* The relevant web is the patterns the chain has touched; saturation is reached when the chain has used the spare capacity of what it touched. This is local, but it passes the detector test only if the capacity of a touched pattern is counted per pattern or per relation (~10²–10⁹ ħ). Counted as free stock at its places, it fails as reading 1 does. The table has no per-pattern capacity. Structure would enter through the headroom before P2's threshold, P_max − P_free ≈ P_max(ε + h), which grows with h; under P6 a chain's quanta in transit are not free, so what they fill is unruled. **Survives as a shape:** N set by the chain reaching the capacity of what it has touched; diamond and coal would differ through h (how much of each place's capacity the material already engages), not through bond counts. The material's own gravitational h is ~10⁻⁴³ per neighbouring atom and ~10⁻²⁷ for a whole millimetre body; outside potentials (Earth 7×10⁻¹⁰, Sun 10⁻⁸, galaxy ~10⁻⁶) dominate and are the same for every material. Binding (P6's draw by frequency) gives h ~ 7×10⁻¹⁰, with diamond and graphite differing by ~2×10⁻¹² (graphite the more bound); binding lowers mass, so its sign is wrong for "more engaged." h matters only if the vacuum's headroom ε is ≲ 10⁻⁹, and then outside potentials dominate and N would depend on location (a seasonal swing ~3×10⁻¹⁰ from Earth's orbit).

**What it gains.** The idea forced a definition, and the arithmetic is firm: no criterion built from free stock at hop-scale places can set N in the window, local or global, because one atom spans ~10²⁴ places. A structure-dependent N needs a capacity per pattern or per relation, not yet in the table. Two translations in this entry are its own and are open for a ruling: "tight" read as near saturation (the author's picture may mean ordered), and "spare capacity" as free stock (P2 suggests headroom).

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** Numbers, citations and the h estimate fixed as above; "reading 3 survives" and "any criterion must be local" were overclaims.

**Grade.** Shape. Readings 1 and 2 excluded as reasons. Reading 3 a form only: its capacity measure is undefined, and with the table's present measures it either fails the detector test or gives a negligible structure effect.

## 2026-11-08 — "a tight web" read as ordered: structure sets how fast a chain grows, not N (shape; direction opposite to the author's picture)

**The author's picture, taken as order.** A stray quantum on a tightly made bed: the whole bed answers at once. Read with "tight" as *ordered* (a crystal) rather than *near saturation* (excluded above as a measure).

**Against the rules as they now stand.** Passages are counted as quanta handed on (ruled). In a harmonic solid, ordered or not, a quantum handed to the vibrations goes into one normal mode: one passage, however many atoms move. In a recoil-free event no quantum is handed on at all; the body takes the momentum as a whole. Many passages come from anharmonic decay, which crystals and glasses both have. Glasses add a bath of two-level systems at low temperature, and a small top band of localised modes; most glass modes are extended but non-propagating. Read this way, the counting makes the stiffer, more harmonic web the less sensitive: its chains stay short. The author's picture makes the tight bed the more sensitive. This reverses the picture and is open for a ruling.

**Standard physics shows something nearby.** Binding stiffness and two-level systems set how readily a solid takes up a quantum; it does not show a count of passages. The Mössbauer recoil-free fraction f = e^{−k²⟨x²⟩} (⟨x²⟩ along k) is large for stiffly bound atoms and at low temperature, small for soft binding; it does not need long-range order (metallic glasses and frozen solutions show it), and nuclear forward scattering is coherent in amorphous samples as well as crystals (nuclear Bragg diffraction is what needs order). Carbon has no Mössbauer transition; diamond's high Debye temperature (~2000 K) is quoted only to show its stiffness. Amorphous solids have an excess of soft modes (the boson peak), a bath of two-level systems below ~1 K (the main decoherence source in superconducting qubits with amorphous oxides), and lower thermal conductivity; mostly their modes are extended but non-propagating (Allen–Feldman diffusons), a localised minority at the top of the spectrum. The real axis is stiffness and anharmonicity, with two-level systems as the one bath unique to glasses; order as such is not the variable.

**What it changes.** The author's diamond-and-coal difference appears, but not as a structure-dependent N: this reading does not need N to vary; structure would set how fast a chain grows toward it. An ordered web keeps a stray quantum's chain short (collective, coherent, counted once); a disordered web multiplies it. The count of passages and the decoherence rate point the same way, but they are different measures (many long-wavelength quanta can carry little which-path information); where they differ in size could be a test. That an isolated body's internal chain would commit is a difference from quantum mechanics that comes from a finite N (and from internal passages counting, unruled), not from this reading. The earlier hope that structure would let N follow from the rules instead of being a constant is not met by this reading: N is still a number the rules do not give.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** The Mössbauer evidence showed stiffness, not order; recoil-free is zero passages, not one; most glass modes are not localised; the conclusion reversed the author's picture while claiming agreement.

**Grade.** Shape: structure would set how fast a chain grows, under the ruled counting. The supporting physics is corrected (stiffness, anharmonicity and two-level systems, not order), and the direction is opposite to the author's picture, owed the ruling.

## 2026-11-08 — the ruling on the web picture: size sets how many stray quanta reach it; stiffness keeps each chain short (shape)

The author's picture, split, keeping the half the evidence and the ruled counting support. *A bigger web is a bigger target: more stray quanta reach it, each a separate chain, so it tips sooner. A stiffer web answers each quantum as one, keeping each chain short, so it holds out longer.* "A tighter web tips sooner" is let go: stiff, ordered materials (diamond in quantum sensing) hold coherence longer and glassy ones (two-level systems in qubit oxides) shorter, and under the counting a web that moves as one hands on one quantum. The author's diamond-and-coal difference survives in the opposite direction: diamond holds out longer. No new rule; N unchanged. Grade: shape, the author's.

## 2026-11-08 — do passages inside an isolated body count toward N? (shape; two readings of a silence)

**The question.** Under the stopping-number shape, a chain of passages through channels becomes a commitment at N. Inside an isolated body quanta are handed on all the time (anharmonic phonon decay, internal conversion, vibrational redistribution in a hot molecule, ~10¹² s⁻¹ per mode). Do those passages count?

**The table has no "inside."** The table's one composite-pattern notion is the three-tile shape (one pattern, internal kinships, tiles committing together); on it, exchanges among parts of one pattern change neither its rate nor its transit, so they reach no channel in C8's sense and are kinships, not passages. Including internal passages needs internal modes to be patterns; excluding them needs a rule for what makes parts one pattern. Neither is in the table: silence. Both readings are carried below.

**Consequence: isolated bodies commit internally, and that does not spoil their interference.** If internal passages count and N measures chain breadth (C8 allows coherent passages, so N cannot count passages over time), an internal chain is capped by the body's mode or quanta count (C₇₀: 204 modes; a 25 kDa molecule: ~6000). Whether a body commits internally then depends on N against that cap. The shape also defines N only for a chain set off by one quantum; a thermal bath has none. A commitment resolves the engaged pattern and conditions what shares a joint amplitude with it (Lüders, ruled). In quantum mechanics internal and centre-of-mass coordinates separate exactly for a free body (apart from the mass-energy coupling behind Pikovski decoherence, which in CO is the clock rule). In CO a commitment is at a place, and C8 draws the place from |a|²; an internal commitment on one arm resolves the path. Molecule interferometry (0.1–1 µm separations, ms flights, molecules internally at hundreds of K) therefore bounds the reading: if internal passages count and commitments fix a place, N exceeds the internal chain size of the largest interfering molecule, ~10³–10⁴, above the polarisation-echo bound. Recoil from thermal photon emission is the observed coupling to the centre of mass; it is an external passage and decoheres as in quantum mechanics (Hackermüller 2004). Grating phases depend on internal polarizability, so internal and centre-of-mass states are correlated at the gratings. Owed: the basis an internal commitment resolves. Place at hop resolution gives ~2×10¹¹ eV per electron event (relativistic: ħc/ℓ) and is excluded by collapse-model heating and X-ray bounds; place at a coarser length is a collapse model and bounded by the same data; the channel's frequency and direction (energy/mode basis) does not heat, and resolving decay products after hand-off leaves the parent's decay rate unchanged.

**Where it differs from quantum mechanics, and where it does not.** A cat state of one mode (Bild 2023; superconducting cavity cats) decoheres by energy loss and dephasing into the rest of the device and its mount; CO, importing the joint law, predicts the same rate, since resolving the bath after hand-off does not change the mode's decay. These are not a test of N. For a truly isolated body (a warm body is not one: it radiates), quantum mechanics keeps the whole pure and reversible; under this reading CO has it commit once an internal chain passes N, a difference visible only by reversal. Reversing a warm body's internal bath is not feasible; the practical tests are engineered many-body echoes (NMR, trapped ions, out-of-time-order echoes on superconducting processors), each to be checked against ruling 3. Polarisation echoes run their forward flip-flop dynamics undriven but reverse them with rf; whether that reversal only steers is owed, as is a citation for ~10–10² (Zhang–Meier–Ernst 1992; Levstein–Usaj–Pastawski 1998).

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** The table does have a composite notion (three tiles); separability of internal and centre-of-mass coordinates is quantum mechanics', not CO's, since a CO commitment is at a place; molecule interferometry is therefore the better bound, not a consistency check; the cats test nothing here; polarisation-echo reversal is rf-driven.

**Grade.** Shape. Internal passages counting is one of two readings of the table's silence (the other: internal exchanges are kinships). Owed: whether N counts breadth or cumulative passages; the resolution basis of an internal commitment; whether an internal commitment fixes a place. If it does, molecule interferometry bounds N at ~10³–10⁴. Lüders conditioning: the ruling (candidate).

## 2026-11-08 — two rulings on the stopping number (the author's, candidate)

4. *N counts a chain's breadth*, not passages accumulated over time. Owed: whether breadth counts distinct quanta or distinct patterns reached; how it is taken in a superposition of chains (per branch, mean or maximum); and whether it is the largest support reached or the current one. C8 already allows coherent repeated passage (an atom and a cavity trading one quantum back and forth); a cumulative count would make that a commitment, which experiment excludes. The NMR bound counts patterns (correlated spins), not quanta: under quanta counting (ruling 2) a polarisation echo has breadth one, and the ~10–10² lower bound no longer applies; many-quanta reversals (spin-mixing SU(1,1) echoes, Linnemann et al. 2016, to be checked under ruling 3) are needed.

5. *A commitment resolves in its channel's basis: the frequency and direction the channel matches.* It happens at a place (the Commitment primitive), and it fixes the engaged pattern's place only as finely as the engager's channel is localised: which engager takes the quantum is drawn from |a|² at the engagers' places, weighted by the coupling (Glauber's photodetection rate, imported with the coupling). A pixel, a grain or a bubble-chamber atom resolves position this way; a channel spread over a whole body does not. The channel basis is the interaction Hamiltonian's pointer basis (einselection), imported with the coupling; CO adds that resolution in it is an event, and when (N), not which basis. Reasons: resolving place at hop resolution (ℓ ≈ 10⁻¹⁸ m) would give a momentum spread ħ/ℓ ≈ 200 GeV/c, ~2×10¹¹ eV per electron event, excluded by collapse-model heating and X-ray bounds; resolving place at a coarser length is a collapse model with a rate, bounded but not excluded by the same data, so the choice against it is the author's; resolving in a basis that commutes with the energy (internal energy states) heats nothing and leaves a parent's decay rate unchanged. Consequence: an internal commitment in a free body does not give away its centre-of-mass path unless the channel's direction does (thermal photon recoil, external, as observed), Molecule interferometry is then consistent whichever way the internal-passages question is decided, provided the place of an internal commitment is an independent draw that records nothing, as the dents are (Entanglement paragraph); that posit, and the dents' open no-signalling cost, are carried with it. Cost: "at a place" and "draws the place from |a|²" (C8) now say where the event is, not what it resolves; the plain doc's C8 wording is amended to say so.

Left open: whether exchanges inside one pattern are passages or kinships (tied to the tiles-and-kinship question).

**Cold reading of rulings 4 and 5, same day; corrected.** Ruling 5 is coherent only with place coming from *which engager* took the quantum (Glauber's rate, imported); "frequency and direction" carry no position. It does not solve the preferred-basis problem: the basis is einselection's, imported with the coupling. For an internal commitment, the independent-draw posit must be extended or the molecule bound returns. Ruling 4 is not yet defined (quanta or patterns; superposition; largest or current), and under quanta counting the NMR polarisation-echo bound no longer bounds N. Heating figure corrected to ~2×10¹¹ eV (the non-relativistic formula was outside its range).

## 2026-11-08 — two more rulings on the measurement shape (the author's, candidate)

6. *Breadth is counted in quanta* (distinct quanta a chain has reached), not in patterns. Consistent with ruling 2; counting patterns would readmit the resonator exclusion (one phonon moving 10¹³–10¹⁷ atoms). Cost, stated: the NMR polarisation-echo bound (patterns) no longer bounds N; a lower bound needs a reversal that hands on many quanta (spin-mixing SU(1,1) echoes and the like, checked under ruling 3). Until then N's lower bound is weak. Still owed: how breadth is taken in a superposition of chains, and whether it is the largest reached or the current.

7. *The independent-draw posit extends to commitments inside a body:* the place of an internal commitment is an independent draw that records nothing about where the body is, as the free-stock dents are. One posit used twice. With it, molecule interferometry is consistent whichever way the internal-passages question is decided; it carries the dents' open no-signalling cost.

**Where the measurement ladder rests.** C8 (a commitment may engage a quantum in transit), through a channel, the place given by which localised engager takes it (coupling and basis imported), outside a channel none; resolution conditions what shares a joint amplitude (Lüders, imported); an engagement is a commitment when the chain of passages it sets off reaches a stopping number N, counted in quanta, by breadth, with drives counting only when they steer; N's value is not in the rules (a fourth constant if adopted), bounded below weakly and above by a macroscopic record; emergent definiteness excluded by premise. Open: whether exchanges inside one pattern are passages or kinships; N's value, which only experiment can move.

## 2026-11-08 — the moving clock as one budget of hops (the author's picture; entered in the plain doc)

*Ticking and travelling share one budget of hops.* The walk hops at c; commitments are its turns; a travelling pattern turns less often per lab second and renews at ω/γ (the covariant intensity (mc²/ħ)ψ̄ψ integrates to ω/γ). This is special relativity's time dilation in the walk's terms (Feynman's checkerboard; "everything moves through spacetime at c"). In a circular orbit, v² = GM/r, so the kinematic slowing v²/2c² is half the gravitational GM/rc². No new rule; a sentence in the plain doc.

## 2026-11-08 — note: "transverse = light" is loose

The identification of light with the transverse part of the which-way field is loose: the transverse field also carries a moving charge's bound magnetic and velocity fields and the Breit/Darwin exchange (this record already notes the continuum limit drops transverse exchange). Light is the emitted (radiation) part; the bound/emitted split is not yet stated in CO (gravity record, the self parts).

## 2026-11-08 — the sea: does the branched-field ruling permit a route through a pair? (silence; the posit a shape; after cold reading)

**Why it is asked.** The gravity record's self-parts entry rests on closure of elementary patterns. Without a sea of pairs, the lattice self-energy (≈ 0.4 GeV ≈ 10³ mₑ at ℓ ≈ 10⁻¹⁸ m, entry of 2026-11-07, further corrections) makes a measured mₑ need a core of about −0.4 GeV; with a sea, and on a walk that keeps chiral symmetry, the self-energy is tamed to a logarithm (Weisskopf 1939). Cost (iv) of the two-pattern ruling (Brown–Ravenhall, handled by a no-pair projection) is the same question from the matter side.

**Two readings of a sea.**
- *As spontaneous events* (pairs happening in empty space around every charge): blocked (P2: an empty place commits only over capacity, and today's vacuum is under; a quantum free stock is excluded, vacuum spread). Pairs from light, which the Charge paragraph states, are events at a non-empty place; no rule yet makes them (C8 changes an existing engager's rate or transit; it starts no new renewing pair). The pair rule is a selection rule with no creation rule behind it.
- *As routes* (QED's virtual pairs, terms in an amplitude, not happenings): a route between commitments that passes through configurations with a pair added.

**Read against the rules.** The branched-field ruling's configurations are those of the patterns present; P8 is silent on number. **The ruling as it stands is silent on a route through a pair:** no rule adds a pattern on a route. Number does change at commitments (C8 ends an absorbed quantum; the Charge paragraph states pair creation from light, without a rule that makes it). The plain doc's force-range sentence (pion exchange: a carrier with mass, no energy to spare, lasting about one renewal time) is already a route through a configuration with a pattern added; it uses this silence informally. Two things come close: the walk has both bands, the antiparticle being the same pattern with the opposite sign (Brown–Ravenhall is the two bands mixing); and totals of every sign cannot change, the selection rule a pair branch needs.

**The posit that would fill it.** *A route between configurations may pass through configurations with a pair added, at the place of the pattern it dresses, totals of every sign conserved; P8 adds all routes that end at one configuration, whatever they passed through.* That is the state space of Fock space (Dirac 1930 to QED), without three things, owed:
- the vertex that gives amplitude to enter a pair configuration (QED's ψ̄γ^μψA_μ, strength e = √(4πα)); the emission coupling of the electron to light is already owed (two-pattern ruling, cold reading);
- the photon sector (the self-energy and vacuum polarisation run through light lines; whether the which-way field is quantised is not ruled);
- the exchange sign.
Also owed: the extension of the Born identification to configurations of varying number; and whether the flips on a pair route's internal lines (each flip identified with a commitment in the plain doc) are amplitudes or commitments.

**What it would and would not pay.**
1. *Closure: not on CO's walk.* With pair routes, the exchange sign and the vertex, the continuum self-energy is Weisskopf's δm/m = (3α/2π) ln(ħ/(mₑcℓ)) ≈ 4–5% for ℓ ≈ 10⁻¹⁸ m (the coefficient 3/2 is the covariant one, transverse photons included, not yet CO's). The logarithm needs chiral symmetry; CO's Wilson walk breaks it, and an additive shift of order (α/4π)·ħc/ℓ times a lattice coefficient (tens at Wilson r = 1), roughly GeV, comes back: closure becomes a tuning (lattice QCD's critical mass), not paid. A chirally symmetric walk (Ginsparg–Wilson, overlap type) would pay it; CO does not have one.
2. *Brown–Ravenhall.* The no-pair projection becomes an approximation to a full number-changing treatment, as in QED, once the vertex exists.
3. *Statistics.* With pair routes the many-pattern space is CO's. If the Born identification extends to it, positive norm follows from possibility ≥ 0, retiring the 11-06 objection to that premise. The exchange sign is still an input: on a strictly local lattice locality selects nothing, and no lattice spin–statistics theorem exists. Weisskopf's logarithm needs that sign (in hole theory the electron blocks sea states; charged spin-0 particles diverge quadratically), so debt 1 depends on it.
4. *Past 1930.* Vacuum polarisation needs the which-way field, not only patterns, to have pair configurations (the Uehling term dresses a static source's field), and the vertex.

**A reading of the posit: no vacuum bubbles.** Every pair route is part of a connected route whose ends are commitments; a pair route with none (QED's disconnected vacuum diagrams) is not a route of anything. In QED these cancel from every normalised amplitude, so nothing measured in flat space changes; in content this is normal ordering, stated as a rule. So the electron sea adds no vacuum energy (at the hop length the free zero-point would be of order ħc/ℓ⁴ ≈ 10⁴⁷ J/m³, ~10⁵⁵–10⁵⁷ times the observed dark energy). The light field's zero-point, if light is quantised, and vacuum condensates (QCD's quark condensate) are not addressed. "Λ from data" is not threatened by the electron sea. (The Casimir force can be computed as a force between the plates' charges, Jaffe 2005, in QED with a quantised photon field, which CO has not supplied.) The dressing gravitates through the measured mass, by closure.

**An exposure found on the way: Lorentz violation from loops.** CO's lattice has a cubic space and a time step. Loops with a Lorentz-violating cutoff give low-energy Lorentz violation of order α/π, not suppressed by powers of ℓ (Collins, Pérez, Sudarsky, Urrutia, Vucetich, PRL 93, 191301 (2004)); bounds on the electron are 10⁻¹⁵ or tighter. Any posit that brings loops onto CO's lattice (this one included) meets this bound; the sea does not cure it. It is recorded against the lattice itself, not only against closure.

**Grade.** Reading: the branched-field ruling is silent on a route through a pair (silence); the plain doc's force-range sentence already uses such a route informally. The posit that would fill it is a **shape**: it gives the configuration space, not the vertex that gives amplitude to enter it. With the vertex and the exchange sign, Brown–Ravenhall and vacuum polarisation follow as in QED, by identification. Closure does not follow on CO's Wilson walk (additive mass shift; O(α) Lorentz violation from the lattice cutoff). It does not yet meet the cascade standard.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** Silence, not exclusion; the rules already change number at commitments; the force-range sentence uses the silence; "Fock space" narrowed to its state space (vertex, photon sector, exchange sign owed); closure fails on the Wilson walk (chiral symmetry); the S1 claim restated (positive norm gained conditionally, exchange sign still an input; debt 1 depends on it); no vacuum bubbles is normal ordering as a rule, not CO-own; vacuum polarisation needs field pair configurations; the Collins et al. exposure added; grade from candidate ruling to shape.

## 2026-11-08 — Lorentz invariance on CO's lattice: tree-level bounds on the hop and the walk, and the loop exposure (after cold reading)

**Light, on a lattice.** For a continuous-time nearest-neighbour lattice field (a Hamiltonian lattice Maxwell field, if light is discretised that way), ω = (2c/ℓ) sin(kℓ/2) gives a group velocity ≈ c[1 − (kℓ)²/8]: subluminal, quadratic. In the parametrisation Δv/c = (3/2)(E/E_QG,2)², ℓ ≈ √12 ħc/E_QG,2 along an axis (6ħc/E_QG,2 along the body diagonal). If light carries a time step (leapfrog), ω ≈ ck[1 + (c²τ²k² − ℓ²Σk_j⁴/k²)/24]: direction-dependent, vanishing along the diagonal at the stability limit cτ = ℓ/√3. Whether light carries a time step is not ruled.
- *Largest momentum.* A lattice has a largest momentum, πħ/ℓ: at 6 × 10⁻¹⁹ m about 1 TeV in the lattice frame (taken near the CMB frame; Earth's motion through it changes nothing at this order), while photons are observed to 1.4 PeV (LHAASO 2021) and cosmic-ray electrons to tens of TeV.
- *Photon time of flight.* LHAASO's GRB 221009A gives E_QG,2 > 6 × 10⁻⁸ E_Pl ≈ 7 × 10¹¹ GeV (subluminal, 95%; PRL 133, 071501 (2024)), so ℓ ≲ 10⁻²⁷ m for light on the lattice.

**The walk is a different object.** The 1D massless Dirac walk is exactly linear, ω = ck to the zone edge; it bends only through what is added to it. Small-k algebra (the reader's, not yet run):
- *Wilson term* (1D reduction, cos ω = cos θ(k) cos k, θ = m + r(1 − cos k), lattice units): ω² ≈ m² + k²(1 + r m) + (r²/4)k⁴. Two effects. A dimension-four term for massive patterns, c_eff² = c²(1 + r mcℓ/ħ) (Wilson fermions' rest mass and kinetic mass differ at order mℓ): the electron's limiting speed exceeds light's by about r mₑcℓ/2ħ, and heavier patterns more. And a superluminal quadratic term, (r²/8)(kℓ)².
- *Sequential x-then-y-then-z step:* a first-order energy shift δω = ±cℓ k_xk_yk_z/|k|, sign by chirality, linear in energy (n = 1); the cubic lattice's parity does not remove it, only a palindromic step does.
- *Light's speed equal to the walk's c* is a ruling, not a reading: with sliding only relabelling, the ratio of the E² and B² coefficients in the Maxwell cost (light's speed) is free, and the Charge paragraph's "unique quadratic cost is Maxwell's" needs boost invariance for that ratio.

**Electron bounds, stronger than light's.** The Crab's 1.1 PeV photon implies electrons of about 2.3 PeV (Li & Ma, PLB 829, 137034 (2022)): dimension-four electron speed excess |δ_e| ≲ 5 × 10⁻²⁰; superluminal quadratic E_LV,2 > 2 × 10¹⁶ GeV; linear η ≲ 1.3 × 10⁻⁷ (vacuum Cherenkov: classical, no loops; needs the charge coupling, candidate). Applied to the walk at Wilson r = 0.3:
- Wilson dimension-four term: r(mₑc/ħ)ℓ < 5 × 10⁻²⁰ gives ℓ ≲ 6 × 10⁻³² m. (At ℓ = 10⁻²⁷ m the electron's Cherenkov threshold would be ≈ 18 TeV, against PeV electrons.)
- Wilson quadratic term: ℓ ≲ 2ħc/(|r|E_LV,2) ≈ 7 × 10⁻³² m, if the 3D sign is superluminal relative to light.
- Sequential ordering: η ≈ 0.4 ℓ/ℓ_P against η ≲ 10⁻⁷, so ℓ below the Planck length: **the sequential ordering is excluded at any ℓ above the Planck length; the palindromic step is needed.**

**What follows for the constants.** With q = P_max, ℓ² = 4πℓ_P²P_max/δ:
- at ℓ ≲ 10⁻²⁷ m (light): (ℓ/ℓ_P)² ≲ 3 × 10¹⁵, P_max/δ ≲ 3 × 10¹⁴ (units of ħ), δ ≳ 4 × 10⁻¹⁵ P_max/ħ;
- at ℓ ≲ 6 × 10⁻³² m (electron, conditional on the 3D computation): (ℓ/ℓ_P)² ≲ 1.4 × 10⁷, δ ≳ 10⁻⁶ P_max/ħ.
Knock-ons: `MATTER.md` lines 21–25 (heaviest pattern m_max = ħ/(ℓc) ≳ 2 × 10¹¹ GeV at 10⁻²⁷ m; "δ ≈ 10⁻³²"), `OPEN_DIRECTIONS.md` doubler line (doublers at ≳ 6 × 10¹⁰ GeV); the lattice self-energy αħc/4ℓ (≳ 4 × 10⁸ GeV); the quadratic-curvature bounds through ℓ (tightened); the "one hop length" entry's P_max < ħ question (changes completely); the vacuum-spread windows (scale with δ, both sides to recheck); the free stock's end; light's coefficient figure.

**Loops.** A Lorentz-violating cutoff, once loops exist, generates species-dependent maximal speeds (dimension four), independent of the cutoff scale and not suppressed by E/E_P (Collins, Pérez, Sudarsky, Urrutia, Vucetich, PRL 93, 191301 (2004), a Yukawa example with a smooth non-invariant cutoff; order α/π for QED by analogy). A Euclidean hypercubic lattice recovers rotation symmetry at dimension four automatically; a real-time lattice with its own time step does not, which is why anisotropic lattice QCD tunes its bare anisotropies. Cubic symmetry makes the generated terms isotropic in the lattice frame; the relevant bounds (isotropic electron c_TT, photon κ̃_tr) reach ~5 × 10⁻²⁰ astrophysically, so untuned α/π ≈ 2 × 10⁻³ needs tuning to ~1 part in 10¹⁷.

**Read against the rules.**
- *The primitives carry no frame.* Time is a local count of commitments; C2 gives each commitment its own clock; the now ruling makes a commitment's now its past cone.
- *The frame comes in with two identifications.* (i) The Dirac walk on a cubic lattice with a global tick. (ii) The network's relations have finite degree (a reading of P2 and R4: degree ≈ P∞/ħ), and no finite-valency graph can be associated with Minkowski spacetime without picking a frame (Bombelli–Henson–Sorkin, MPLA 24, 2579 (2009); Dowker–Henson–Sorkin, MPLA 19, 1829 (2004), for the criterion); only structures of infinite valency, such as sprinkled causal sets, avoid it, at the price of non-local dynamics; CO's commitments are not sprinkled (gravity record, causal-set entry, excluded).
- *Nothing in the rules suppresses the loop terms.* "Sliding costs nothing" is relabelling, not boosts; CO has no protecting symmetry (supersymmetry forbids dimension-four Lorentz violation: Groot Nibbelink–Pospelov, PRL 94, 081601 (2005)).
- *A tension.* The walk's global tick makes the now ruling's clause "no preferred frame" false of the substrate as identified; the past-cone relation itself survives, with the lattice's causal cone in place of the light cone (the sequential walk's cone is a cube reaching √3c along the diagonals).

**Scope.** Tree-level CO meets photon time-of-flight once ℓ ≲ 10⁻²⁷ m. It does not yet meet electron tests: on the Wilson walk the electron's limiting speed exceeds light's at dimension four, at tree level, and its dispersion bends superluminally; Crab PeV electrons then need ℓ ≲ 10⁻³¹ m if that holds in 3D. The step to loops adds O(α/π) species-dependent speeds on top. The scope line is not the safety line; the safety line runs through the tree level.

**Ways out, none taken.** (a) A walk without a global tick, matching the now ruling; finite degree is the hard part. (b) A protecting symmetry, not in CO. (c) Tuning, not an answer by the method. (d) Lorentz-invariant discreteness at the price of locality, excluded for CO's commitments as they stand. (e) Lorentz invariance emerging at low energy (Chadha–Nielsen 1983): speeds converge only logarithmically, short of 10⁻²⁰ without supersymmetry or strong coupling. (f) Separation of scales (Pospelov–Shang 2012; Polchinski 2012): suppression (M_LV/Λ)², needing a second scale. (g) A dynamical frame (Einstein-aether, Hořava): makes the tick a field, does not by itself protect matter. (h) A deformed rather than broken symmetry for the walk (Bisio–D'Ariano–Perinotti; Arrighi–Facchini–Forets 2014), in the quantum-walk literature the repo already cites.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** The dispersion used was light's, not the walk's; the walk bends through the Wilson term (dimension four for massive patterns, quadratic superluminal) and the sequential ordering (linear); electron bounds (Li & Ma) are stronger than photon time-of-flight; the sequential ordering is excluded, so the palindrome is needed; light's speed equal to the walk's c is a ruling; "locally finite" corrected to finite valency; tick "contradicts" softened to falsifies the no-preferred-frame clause; "scope line = safety line" withdrawn; ways out completed; citations completed; the loop exposure is on the pair-route shape, not on a posit.

**Grade.**
- Needed (by data): ℓ ≲ 10⁻²⁷ m for light on the lattice; the palindromic step (sequential excluded).
- Candidate bound: ℓ ≲ 10⁻³¹ m from the electron (owed: the 3D walk's dispersion, registrable: its outcome is unknown and changes the ℓ ruling).
- Owed, the author's: whether light's speed equals the walk's c, and whether light carries a time step.
- Loops: the pair-route shape carries an exposure: on the walk as identified it generates O(α/π) isotropic speed differences, to be tuned to ~1 part in 10¹⁷; the untuned version is excluded.
- The now-ruling tension is the CO-own direction to read.

## 2026-11-09 — the global tick against the now ruling: the walk as a circuit of local gates (candidate reading, after cold reading)

**The tension (Lorentz entry).** The walk updates every place on one shared tick, which makes the now ruling's "no preferred frame" false of the substrate as identified. Two questions are tangled in it: (1) does the walk need a global *now* (a preferred slicing)? (2) does it pick a preferred *frame*?

**(1) The tick is a slicing, not part of the walk.** Each partial shift P_j moves the α_j = +1 part one place forward and the α_j = −1 part one place back, line by line; on each line it is a one-dimensional walk of flow 2 − 2 = 0, and a one-dimensional walk of flow zero is a finite stack of layers of unitaries on neighbouring places, with no extra cells (Gross, Nesme, Vogts, Werner, CMP 310, 419 (2012)); the lines are independent, so P_j is such a stack in 3D. The mass flip exp(−iβθ) acts place by place. The Wilson flip exp(−iβ[θ + rΣ_j(1 − cos k_j)]) does not: it couples every place to every other, with weights falling off faster than exponentially, so the adopted walk is local only up to those tails; a strictly local Wilson walk is owed, and with it a recheck of L1's coefficient. (The plain doc's C6, "changes a pattern's internal state without moving it", is false for the Wilson flip.) In a stack of local gates, gates neither of which lies in the other's past act on separate places and commute, so every ordering compatible with the graph of dependencies gives the same walk: the outcome depends on that graph, not on a slicing of it into ticks (for causal sets, Rideout–Sorkin's discrete general covariance, PRD 61, 024002 (2000)). The graph belongs to the decomposition; only the walk's cone is decomposition-independent. The global tick is one way of drawing the circuit.

If the flip gates are the commitments (a posit: a gate acts at every place each step, coherently; a definite event is not yet in the walk, and a pattern's flips are amplitude-weighted corners of paths), a commitment's past cone is its past in that graph, with the lattice's cone (for the palindrome, a cube of half-width 2 places per step, √3c on the diagonals) in place of the light cone; Stein's argument does not apply to that cone. The flips (mass steps), identified with commitments in the plain doc, are gates of the circuit; the hops are possibility moving between them.

**(2) The frame does not go with the tick.** Embedded in Minkowski space, a periodic finite-degree graph has a finite set of edge vectors; the Lorentz transformations that map it to itself form a finite group, and a finite group fixes a timelike direction: a rest frame. For points sprinkled at random, Bombelli–Henson–Sorkin (MPLA 24, 2579 (2009)) show that finite valency still picks a direction. So removing the tick removes the preferred *now* and leaves the preferred *frame*. Collins et al.'s loop terms and L1's tree-level speed shift are properties of the walk's one-step rule, which every slicing shares: both stand.

**What it says.**
- The now ruling and the walk are consistent once the walk is read as a circuit of local gates (candidate reading). "No universal now" in this sense holds for any local lattice rule; it is not relativity's becoming, and the lattice's rows still define a simultaneity that nothing in the dynamics needs.
- The Lorentz exposure is located in the finite valency of the walk's causal structure. The exits that remain: infinite valence (non-local; excluded for CO's commitments as they stand); a protecting symmetry (none in CO); separation of scales (Pospelov–Shang), needing a second scale; a deformed rather than broken symmetry (Bisio, D'Ariano, Perinotti, PRA 94, 042120 (2016), free automata without a Wilson term; scope to confirm from the paper): on the Wilson walk it is already broken at tree level, since L1's massive and massless patterns have different low-energy limiting speeds and a deformed group is ordinary Lorentz at low energy; it would need a common deformation for all species, surviving interactions, and another cure for the doublers. Way (a) of the Lorentz entry is met for the now, not for the frame. Tuning is not counted.
- The now ruling, split. "No universal now": ruled for the primitives; on the walk read as a circuit, a candidate reading (no global update needed; cone the lattice's). "No preferred frame": holds of the primitives; false of the substrate as identified.

**Knock-ons, not yet entered.** `OPEN_DIRECTIONS.md`'s now item ("past light cone", "owes nothing") no longer holds on the substrate. The entanglement item sets the foliation option aside by an exhaustive reading of "no preferred frame"; with a frame the substrate has, that setting-aside is a posit against available structure. C6's "without moving it" (Wilson flip).

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** The circuit argument made on Gross–Nesme–Vogts–Werner (walks, flow zero, no ancillas), not Arrighi–Nesme–Werner (many-body); the Wilson flip is quasi-local, not local (a strictly local Wilson walk owed; C6 affected); flip gates as commitments named a posit; the cone is the lattice's, and "no universal now" there is cheap; the frame argument made elementary (finite symmetry group fixes a timelike direction), BHS for the random case; "regularity" dropped for finite valency; the deformed-symmetry exit is broken at tree level on the Wilson walk by L1; separation of scales restored; the split's grades separated.

**Grade.** Candidate reading: the global tick is a slicing of a circuit of local gates (up to the Wilson flip's tails), and "no universal now" holds on it with the lattice's cone. The preferred frame stands, located in the finite valency of the walk's causal structure. Posits named: flip gates = commitments; the exhaustive reading of the third clause, now against a frame the substrate has. Owed: a strictly local Wilson walk.

## 2026-11-09 — the vertex: the charge clause and a pattern's coupling to the which-way field (shape; two posits proposed, after cold reading)

**Why it is asked.** The sea entry found the pair-route posit gives configurations with pairs but not "the vertex that gives amplitude to enter" them; `MATTER.md` lists "the coupling that gives g = 2" as open; the two-pattern ruling's cold reading noted the electron's coupling to light is not supplied. One object, three debts.

**What the rules say.** Charge (candidate): a relation carries a ratio that reverses with direction, a 1-form; charge is a sign a pattern carries *on the relations it uses*. "Sliding costs nothing" is ruled as relabelling places; the Maxwell cost's uniqueness needs only that the field's cost depend on holonomies (invariance under A → A + dχ acting on A alone). The two-pattern entry checked that the standard many-body coupling is gauge invariant (ψ(x, y) → e^{i(χ(x)+χ(y))}ψ), which adopts, not derives, the action on amplitudes.

**A hidden inconsistency in the charge sector.** The ratio is described as real: r_ji = 1/r_ij, ℓ = ln R, holonomy e^Φ (gravity record and this record's charge entries), whose natural freedom is a rescaling of possibility, P → e^χ P (the group R₊). The quantisation row ("a multiple of 2π") uses a phase (U(1)). The two are not the same object; a real ratio placed on a hop gives S·e^{qA}, which is not unitary (an imaginary vector potential, Hatano–Nelson type). The charge sector has been using U(1) without saying so.

**Two posits that would give the coupling.** (1) The which-way ratio is a phase, r_ij = e^{iA_ij}. (2) The shift A → A + dχ is a redundancy of the whole description, acting on amplitudes as ψ → e^{iqχ}ψ. (The exact part of the real ratio, d ln P, the free-stock gradient, is physical and classical; under (1)–(2) it must not couple to charge, or charged patterns would feel the stock's gradient: stated as a condition.) Given them, a unitary hop that respects the redundancy must carry the link phase:

$$S_j^{+}:\ \psi(x)\to e^{iqA_j(x)}\psi(x)\ \text{at}\ x+\hat e_j;\qquad S_j^{-}:\ \psi(x)\to e^{-iqA_j(x-\hat e_j)}\psi(x)\ \text{at}\ x-\hat e_j,$$

minimal coupling on the lattice (the 2D gauge-coupled quantum walk of Arnault and Debbasch, PRA 93, 052301 (2016); the 3D half-shift Wilson form is the same construction carried over, not taken from the paper). Its continuum limit is Dirac's equation with ∂ → ∂ − iqA, which gives g = 2 in the continuum limit. A₀ enters as an on-site phase per step (as in that paper, and as the two-pattern cold reading put the Coulomb term on the time step).

**The moment.** The charge clause supplies no separate coupling at a place, but read as "no coupling at a place" it would also remove A₀ (the Coulomb coupling, and with it the two-pattern ruling and hydrogen); so either a step at a place counts as a relation, or the clause excludes only an independent on-site σF term. A σF term still arises from hops around a plaquette (Dirac's own g = 2 is the σF part of D̸²); the walk fixes its coefficient. On the Wilson walk an O(ℓ) part is expected (the lattice artefact the clover term removes; Sheikholeslami–Wohlert 1985), the same rest/kinetic split L1b measured, so at tree level g = 2 + O(w·mcℓ/ħ), below 10⁻¹⁸ at the needed ℓ and invisible against a_e (precision about 10⁻¹³). The measured anomaly, a_e = α/2π + …, needs loops, which on CO's lattice meet the Lorentz exposure.

**What it gives the sea.** The hop phase mixes the walk's two bands wherever A varies: in a one-pattern walk, Klein's mixing; it is the amplitude to enter a pair configuration once the walk is second-quantised (vacuum the generator's lowest state, on the C-ruling's branch |ε| < π, which fails in the doubler sector) with the exchange sign (without it, no hole picture: Klein's problem for bosons is pair amplification). Emitting or absorbing light needs the link phase as an operator with conjugate E (the photon sector); with a classical A the hop phase gives only external-field pair production. So the vertex's form is the charge clause's hop phase, under posits (1)–(2). Still owed: the photon sector, the exchange sign, the Born identification for varying number, and the status of flips on internal lines. The hop phase enters pairs wherever A varies, not only at a pattern's place: the pair-route shape's "at the place of the pattern it dresses" must be widened or answered; real pairs made there would still need a C8-type commitment to become events.

**Costs.** Two posits, neither already in the rules: the ratio is a U(1) phase, not the real ratio of possibility; the shift acts on amplitudes. A third premise reads "uses relations" as excluding a separate coupling at a place; it does not stop hops from generating a σF term, and must allow A₀'s on-site phase. The coupling strength, e = √(4πα), is the measured input. The meson couplings of the nuclear force are not touched.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** "Local phase relabelling free" is not already a premise (sliding is relabelling places; the Maxwell uniqueness acts on A alone): two posits named; the charge sector's real ratio against its 2π quantisation named as an inconsistency; the displayed hop's index corrected; A₀ restored (the at-a-place exclusion would remove it); "exactly g = 2" withdrawn (O(ℓ) Wilson artefact, harmless at the needed ℓ); band mixing made conditional on second quantisation, the vacuum, the exchange sign and the photon operator; the citation's scope stated; "the vertex is not a separate object" softened to its form.

**Grade.** Candidate ruling proposed: two posits (the ratio is a U(1) phase; its shift acts on amplitudes). With them, hops carry e^{iqA} (minimal coupling) and g = 2 at tree level up to O(mcℓ/ħ). The vertex is a shape: its form given; photon sector, exchange sign, Born extension and internal flips owed. It cannot grade above the charge sector, itself a candidate.

Ruled on the vertex entry: (1) the which-way quantity a relation carries is a U(1) phase, e^{iA}, not the real ratio of possibility; (2) shifting it by dχ is a redundancy acting on amplitudes as ψ → e^{iqχ}ψ. Both candidate. Entered in `MATTER.md` (Charge; Open: "the coupling that gives g = 2" becomes "the anomalous moment (needs loops)") and the plain doc (Charge). The charge sector's earlier "real ratio, holonomy e^Φ" against "a multiple of 2π" is resolved in favour of the phase; the real-ratio wording in earlier entries is superseded, not deleted.

Under the ruling, a relation carries two different things: the occupancy and the real ratio of possibility, which ratio hopping (P1) compares, and the which-way phase A, which charge couples to. The gravity record's reading that the ratio has rest value d ln P (the "ratio will not do" step that led to occupancy) belonged to the real ratio of possibility.
