## 2026-11-09 — quantised light: what the photon sector would take (shape; a link posit proposed with its costs, after cold reading)

**Why it is asked.** The vertex entry left two items beneath the sea and loops: the exchange sign, and the photon sector (light's quantum states, beyond C8's engagement of light by quanta ħω). The two-pattern ruling's cold reading named the choice: "either a relation carries a conjugate pair (ratio and flux), which is the statement that the ratio has canonical dynamics, or the Coulomb term lives on the time step".

**What is in hand.**
- The which-way quantity on each relation is a U(1) phase, e^{iA} (author's ruling, candidate), with the shift acting on amplitudes.
- Gauss's law on the network: net electric flux out of a place equals the sign there; signs are integers.
- Light is possibility in transit (P6) and is identified with the emitted part of the which-way field.
- The amplitude rule: routes bringing possibility to one place combine by amplitudes (P8). It speaks of routes of a pattern, not of configurations of a field.
- C8 ends an absorbed quantum of light.

**Not a reading.** P8 gives light an amplitude over its routes, whose wavefunction is the classical mode function (Landau–Peierls; Białynicki-Birula): first quantisation. An amplitude over the field's own configurations, Ψ[A] over the phase on every relation, is a further statement: a second level, with a conjugate flux. Light also has two homes in the rules: as possibility in transit it is an amount (classical by the scope ruling); as the emitted part of the which-way field it is an excitation of the phase and its flux.

**The posit.** *Each relation carries an amplitude over its which-way phase, A ∈ [0, 2π); so a conjugate flux E = −i∂/∂A with [E, U] = U (U = e^{iA}), and physical states satisfy Gauss's law, G_x|ψ⟩ = 0.* With single-valued wavefunctions E has integer eigenvalues, consistent with Gauss's law with integer signs (a non-compact field is too; and quark charges of 1/3 would force a flux unit e/3, with the electron carrying three, which does not sit with "a sign"). This is the compact lattice field of Kogut and Susskind (PRD 11, 395 (1975)): H = (g²/2)ΣE² + (1/g²)Σ_p(1 − cos Φ_p), in lattice units. In 2+1 dimensions compact U(1) confines at every coupling (Polyakov, Nucl. Phys. B120, 429 (1977)); in 3+1 it has a Coulomb (photon) phase at weak coupling (Guth 1980; Fröhlich–Spencer 1982), separated from a confining phase near β = 1/g² ≈ 1.01. The measured coupling (e² = 4πα ≈ 0.092, β ≈ 11) is in the photon phase: a consistency condition met, not derived. Its low-lying states are oscillator modes of the transverse field, quanta of ħω, with the lattice Laplacian's dispersion ω² = (4c²/ℓ²)Σ sin²(k_jℓ/2), not the walk's. The posit contains the two-pattern ruling's branching as its Gauss-law sector: it quantises every relation, and the Coulomb part comes out of the constraint, so no bound/emitted split is needed for the construction. One posit would replace "branched with its sources" and fill the photon-sector silence.

**What comes with it, without a posit beyond the link's.**
- *Every photon number.* The count is the occupation of the field's modes, so states of 0, 1, 2, … quanta are in the state space; changing it takes the hop's coupling (rulings (1)–(2) plus this posit). The vertex moves between them unitarily, as routes; C8, where it applies, makes one absorption an event (emission is unitary, atom–field entanglement, not an event).
- *Light's exchange sign.* A wavefunction over a product of link phases is a bosonic representation: settled for light by this construction's choice of representation (commutators), not forced (supporting: a compact group variable has no sensible anticommuting quantisation; Pauli 1940).
- *The vertex as an operator,* for a fixed number of patterns (a Pauli–Fierz-like one-electron sector): U contains a + a†, so the hop phase is emission and absorption; each hop moves one unit of flux, so Gauss's law is kept exactly. Pair processes still need the electrons' exchange sign and the pair-route posit.

**Costs.**
- *A new posit on P7.* A relation carries a phase and its conjugate flux, with Gauss's law on physical states.
- *The link space is infinite.* E ∈ ℤ is unbounded: against GNVW's finite cells (the circuit reading of the tick), against finite capacity P_max, and against "no infinities". The finite option (quantum link models, Chandrasekharan–Wiese, NPB 492, 455 (1997)) makes U non-unitary.
- *Zero-point energy.* Under light's draw by its mean, the zero-point would draw at about ħc/ℓ⁴, roughly 10¹⁰⁷ above the observed vacuum density at the needed ℓ: normal ordering for the photon field is needed, not optional (the cosmological-constant problem returning). Light's draw must be restated as ⟨:T^μν:⟩ (|a|² has no referent in a Fock state), which can be locally negative (squeezed light, Casimir; Ford–Roman).
- *Loops are live.* A quantised photon plus a charged walk on a frame-picking lattice gives species-dependent maximal speeds at O(α/π) (Collins et al. 2004), needing tuning of about 10¹⁷ against electron–photon bounds: from an exposure to a consequence.
- *Hamiltonian against the walk.* A continuous-time Hamiltonian has no strict light cone (a Lieb–Robinson bound), the walk has one; light at the walk's c becomes a matching condition at tree level and in loops; a stepped coupled field is owed (prior art only in 1+1, with no photons: Arrighi, Bény, Farrelly, QIP 19, 88 (2020)).
- *Monopoles.* Compact lattice U(1) carries Dirac monopoles of mass ~ħc/(αℓ), about 10¹⁷–10¹⁸ GeV at the needed ℓ; with the hot beginning, a monopole problem unless something dilutes them.
- *C8 and Fock modes.* Annihilating a mode lowers its occupation everywhere at once, which bears on C8's open question of where the possibility elsewhere goes and sits uneasily with possibility at places.

**What is left beneath the sea.** The exchange sign for patterns; the Born identification for varying number; the status of flips on internal lines.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** The central step is a posit, not a reading (P8 is about routes; an amplitude over field configurations is second quantisation); the posit contains the branching, so no bound/emitted split is needed; Hamiltonian coefficient fixed; Polyakov restated (2+1 confines at all couplings; 3+1 has a Coulomb phase); integer flux consistent with, not required by, Gauss's law; emission is unitary, not C8's reverse; bosons built in, not forced; costs added: the infinite link space against "no infinities" and P_max, zero-point at 10¹⁰⁷ under light's draw, loops now live, the Hamiltonian–walk mismatch, monopoles, C8 and global modes.

**Grade.** Shape, with a candidate ruling proposed: each relation carries an amplitude over its which-way phase (conjugate flux E, [E, U] = U, Gauss's law on states). With it the standard compact lattice field follows (integer fluxes, photons as quanta ħω, every photon number, bosonic exchange built in, the vertex as an operator for fixed pattern number). Its costs include a conflict with "no infinities" (the link space) and the zero-point's draw, which needs photon normal ordering at once. It cannot grade above the charge sector (candidate).

## 2026-11-09 — the light posit against "no infinities" (the author's stance)

The author holds "no infinities" hard. The link posit as stated (an amplitude over the full circle, flux E ∈ ℤ unbounded) gives each relation an infinite state space: **excluded by the principle**, as stated.

**What the principle leaves.** A finite flux per relation, capped by capacity (P2: a place holds at most P_max; a relation's flux capped at some N_max units). That is the quantum link model's shape (Chandrasekharan–Wiese 1997): the link carries a finite spin, E = S_z with |E| ≤ S, and U = S₊ raises the flux by one. Costs: U is no longer unitary (a raising operator), so the hop phase of the vertex entry holds only for fluxes below the cap; the U(1) field is recovered for large S or by dimensional reduction, not exactly. A field-strength cap is the old idea of Born and Infeld (1934), whose motive was a finite electron self-energy: the same "no infinities" reason. Observationally harmless at the needed hop: a flux of one unit through a relation of area ℓ² is e/(ε₀ℓ²) ≈ 10⁵⁴ V/m at ℓ = 1.3 × 10⁻³¹ m, a field scale near ħc/ℓ ≈ 10¹⁵ GeV, far above the light-by-light bound on a Born–Infeld scale (about 100 GeV; Ellis, Mavromatos and You 2017).

**Grade.** The unbounded link posit: excluded by "no infinities". The capped link (flux bounded by capacity): shape, the CO-shaped direction for light's quantum states, with its own costs (a non-unitary U at the cap; U(1) only approximately; the zero-point, loops, monopoles and the walk mismatch as before, the zero-point now finite per relation but still needing normal ordering).

## 2026-11-09 — the stopping number N against data (shape, after cold reading)

**Why it is asked.** An unanchored cold reading (README and plain doc only) named, as the step that would most raise CO's standing, making C8 a rule that gives one number standard quantum mechanics leaves open. The measurement ladder names a candidate: an engagement is a commitment when the chain of passages it sets off reaches breadth N, counted in quanta (rulings 4, 6); with N physical, reversal fails beyond it. Quantum mechanics has no such number; it asserts reversal at every breadth. Where do data reach it?

**Where reversal at large breadth is done.** Solid-state NMR grows correlated clusters with a double-quantum Hamiltonian and reads them out by reversing it, a 90° phase shift turning H_DQ into −H_DQ (multiple-quantum coherence, MQC; Baum, Munowitz, Garroway and Pines, J. Chem. Phys. 83, 2015 (1985)). In adamantane, clusters up to K̄ ≈ 4900 spins are made and read out this way; their decoherence rate grows as K̄^p with p ≈ 0.48, with no break from K ≈ 10 to 5000 (Krojanski and Suter, PRA 74, 062319 (2006); earlier, PRL 93, 090501 (2004)): a statement about decoherence rate, not about how much signal returns. Without perturbation clusters grow without limit over the times measured; with perturbation they saturate at a localisation size that shrinks as the perturbation grows (Álvarez, Suter and Kaiser, Science 349, 846 (2015)). Loschmidt echoes on the same system recover information scrambled among ~10² spins and show a floor on the echo's decay rate, 1/T₃ ≈ (0.15 ± 0.02)/T₂, independent of the perturbation's size (Sánchez, Chattah, Wei, Buljubasich, Cappellaro and Pastawski, PRL 124, 030601 (2020)); no size dependence of the floor is reported.

**What that says about N, under the rulings.**
- *Counted in quanta,* with drives counting only when they steer (ruling 3, which names double-quantum NMR), the double-quantum sequences do not count: the rf field supplies the two quanta of every pair flip, in the forward and the reversed evolution alike. The 2020 echoes are pulse-sequence-made too. Under rulings 3 and 6 these reversals therefore do not bound N, and the ladder's "bounded below weakly" stands. Read past ruling 3, the coherence-order width (σ = √(K/2) ≈ 50 at K ≈ 5000) is a bra–ket difference in a nearly maximally mixed state, not a count of quanta handed on; no map from K or M to quanta is in the rules. (The NMR seed is a term of a mixed state, not a quantum in transit: the 11-08 caveat stands.) Other large echoes (≈100 trapped ions, Gärttner et al. 2017; ≈65 superconducting qubits, 2025) are drive- or gate-made and meet ruling 3 the same way.
- *Counted in patterns,* the MQC reversals would put N above ~5 × 10³; the 2020 floor, at ~10² correlated spins, then cannot be N.
- *Scaling only.* If reversal fails once breadth reaches N and breadth grows at a rate ∝ 1/T₂, the floor is ∝ 1/T₂, as observed; standard accounts (the perturbation's effect amplified by scrambling) give the same scaling.

**The signature.** N predicts a ceiling in reversed cluster breadth that does not move when either the perturbation or the coupling is scaled. Standard accounts give ceilings that move with the perturbation (localisation, Álvarez–Suter–Kaiser 2015) and otherwise smooth loss. No such fixed ceiling is seen: K grows to ~5000 (2006) and without limit below the critical perturbation (2015), and a floor at ~10² correlated spins (2020) cannot be N in a count that also lets ~5000-spin clusters be reversed.

**What it gives.** The stopping-number shape, not C8 as written, would give a number: N, the breadth in quanta beyond which reversal fails. If adopted, that would be CO's first departure from quantum mechanics, which allows reversal at every size. It would place CO with the triggered-threshold reduction models (nothing for a pattern left alone; a non-unitary step for a chain past N), and the README's "predicts nothing of its own" and the plain doc's "Not yet" line would need to change with it; if internal passages count, an isolated body would commit once its internal chain passes N, against "a pattern left alone is not resolved". Stated as a shape.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** Ruling 3 already excludes double-quantum NMR, so these data do not bound N under rulings 3 and 6 (the drafted "10² to 10³·⁷ quanta" withdrawn); the coherence-order width is not a quanta count; the knee contrast narrowed (standard theory has perturbation-dependent ceilings; N's is fixed), "hidden in the readout" deleted; Álvarez–Suter–Kaiser 2015 added; "a number QM leaves open" corrected to "a number QM does not have"; N named as CO's first departure from QM and placed with the threshold models; the plain doc's C8 contains no N, so the number is the shape's, not C8's.

**Grade.** Shape. Data in reach do not bound N under rulings 3 and 6 (the drive is a partner in every pair flip); read in patterns, they would put N above ~5 × 10³, which the 2020 floor (~10² spins) then cannot be. Owed: a reversal that hands on many quanta with a drive that only steers (still none found); whether the 2020 floor carries a size dependence; whether internal passages count, before "a pattern left alone is not resolved" is leaned on beside N.

## 2026-11-09 — lower bounds on N from reversals where the drive only steers (literature check; shape)

**The search.** Reversals of spreading carried by the systems' own interactions (energy-conserving flip-flops, exchange, photon hopping), with any drive used only to flip the sign of the Hamiltonian or to steer, not to supply quanta at each step (ruling 3), and breadth read in quanta handed on (ruling 6).

| Experiment | Breadth before reversal | Drive | Recovery | Bounds N? |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Zhang, Meier, Ernst, PRL 69, 2149 (1992), polarisation echo, ferrocene | ~10 spins by natural flip-flops (estimate) | forward leg free; reverse leg under spin-lock rf | large at short times, fading with reversal time | weakly (N ≳ 10) |
| Levstein, Usaj, Pastawski, JCP 108, 2718 (1998); Usaj et al. (1998), metallocenes | ~10–30 spins (estimate) | as above | partial, gradual | weakly |
| Sánchez, Chattah, Wei, Buljubasich, Cappellaro, Pastawski, PRL 124, 030601 (2020), scaled dipolar echo, adamantane | ~10² correlated spins (spread ∝ (δt)^1.49) | natural flip-flops between pulses; pulse trains scale and flip the sign; double-quantum terms not used | recovered at ~10² spins; decay floor 1/T₃ ≈ 0.15/T₂ | **N ≳ 10², if the pulse trains count as steering** |
| Rhim, Pines, Waugh, PRB 3, 684 (1971), magic echo, CaF₂ | many spins, not counted | rf-dressed reversal | large, incomplete | marginal |
| Braumüller et al., Nature Phys. 18, 172 (2022), 3 × 3 transmons | photons hopping naturally over 9 sites | sign flip by frame changes and detunings: steering only | 91.5% at 70 ns | N ≳ a few |
| Geier et al., Phys. Rev. Research (2024), Rydberg gas | dipolar exchange among many atoms (count not checked) | encoding change flips the sign: steering | re-magnetisation, limited by motion | probably; full text owed |
| Linnemann et al., PRL 117, 013001 (2016), BEC spin mixing | ~1.4 pairs | steering | partial | no useful bound |

Excluded by ruling 3 (the drive supplies quanta at each step): double-quantum NMR (Álvarez–Suter–Kaiser 2015; Krojanski–Suter), laser-driven ion echoes (Gärttner et al. 2017), gate-based qubit echoes (Mi et al. 2021).

**The strongest defensible bound.** From Sánchez et al. (2020): passing correlations among K spins by pairwise flip-flops takes at least K − 1 passes, so about 10² quanta were handed on and the reversal still recovered signal: **N ≳ 10²**, conditional on two readings. (i) *The drive.* The pulse trains briefly rotate every spin, exchanging Zeeman quanta with the field with zero net exchange per cycle; whether that is "steering" under ruling 3 is the author's to rule. Under the strict reading only the transmon echo is beyond dispute, N ≳ a few. (ii) *The count.* In high-temperature NMR the excitation is a small excess polarisation, not a single quantum; "10² correlated spins" is a spread of correlations, and its conversion to quanta handed on is an inference, owed with a statement of ruling 6 for mixed states.

**The shape of the loss.** No experiment shows a wall at a fixed breadth. The 2020 floor sets a time scale (T₃ ≈ T₂/0.15) and fades gradually; a stopping number predicts a ceiling in recovered breadth that does not move with the coupling or the noise.

**Grade.** Shape. N ≳ 10² if double-quantum-free NMR pulse trains count as steering (ruled), else N ≳ a few; no sharp breadth wall seen. Owed: the full Geier et al. (2024) figures (the clearest steering-only case at large breadth), ruling 6 stated for mixed states.

Sources: Sánchez et al. 2020 (https://dspace.mit.edu/bitstream/1721.1/124332/2/PhysRevLett.124.030601.pdf); Levstein, Usaj, Pastawski 1998 (https://arxiv.org/abs/cond-mat/9803047); Braumüller et al. 2022 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2102.11751); Geier et al. 2024 (https://arxiv.org/abs/2402.13873); Linnemann et al. 2016 (https://arxiv.org/abs/1602.07505).

## 2026-11-09 — ruling 3 read for pulse trains (ruled, candidate); N ≳ 10² on record; the Rydberg echo read

Ruled: pulses that scale or flip the sign of a natural interaction, with zero net energy exchange per cycle, are steering under ruling 3. So the double-quantum-free NMR echo (Sánchez et al. 2020) counts, and **N ≳ 10²** stands as the bound on record, conditional on reading "~10² correlated spins by flip-flops" as ~10² quanta handed on (ruling 6 for mixed states, owed).

**Geier et al. (2024), full abstract and methods read** (arXiv 2402.13873). ⁸⁷Rb Rydberg atoms at ~11 μK, N = 332 to 1333 atoms at random positions; dipolar flip-flop (XX) exchange, energy-conserving, other pair states detuned; reversal by two π-pulses moving each spin to a second pair of Rydberg states where the coupling changes sign (C₃ → −kC₃, k ≈ 1.03): steering under the ruling just made. Magnetisation relaxes to zero within ~0.7 μs and returns to ~0.15 after reversal; at t_rev = 6 μs about 0.2 remains for the weakest interactions; losses come from finite transfer pulses and atomic motion (coupling changes of ~20% after six interaction cycles). The paper does not measure how many atoms take part or how far correlations spread; its simulations use 16-atom clusters. So it is a clean steering-only reversal among ~10³ atoms, but its breadth in quanta handed on is not measured, and it does not raise the bound past 10² on present evidence. A measurement of correlation spread before reversal in this platform would be the test that could.

## 2026-11-09 — ruling 6 for warm samples: how breadth in quanta reads on a high-temperature spin system (shape, after cold reading)

**The question.** In room-temperature NMR the state is nearly maximally mixed, ρ ≈ 2^{−K}(1 + εΣI_z) with ε ~ 10⁻⁵; the echo tracks a small local excess of polarisation, not a single quantum (the caveat that the NMR seed is "a term of a mixed state, not a quantum in transit"). Ruling 6 counts breadth as distinct quanta a chain has reached. How does that read here, and what does "~10² correlated spins" (Sánchez et al. 2020) give?

**Four assumptions, named.**
- (i) *Posited* (a choice of members, justified by ruling 5's channel basis): the sample is a mixture of definite Zeeman configurations, each evolving unitarily and each reversed by the echo. A nearly maximally mixed state can be split into members in any basis; the Zeeman basis is the channel basis. If the thermal mixture came from contact with the lattice it is an improper mixture in standard quantum mechanics; calling it ignorance needs CO to say those contacts committed it. This answers the mixed-state caveat by positing members.
- (ii) A flip-flop between an up and a down neighbour hands one Zeeman quantum (ħω₀) through a resonant channel, a passage; C8's direction clause is read as void for near-field exchange.
- (iii) Breadth is the support of the evolved disturbance (the "largest support reached" reading of ruling 4's owed items).
- (iv) Flip-flops inside one crystal are passages, not kinships (open; "Where the measurement ladder rests"). If they are kinships, the bound is void.
And ruling 3 as ruled (the pulse trains steer).

**The count.** Quanta are identical, so "distinct" means counted by initial site: the up spins inside the region a disturbance has reached, measured by the multiple-quantum cluster size (a stand-in, across the ensemble, for how far each member's entanglement has spread). At high temperature about half the spins are up, so a disturbance reaching K spins has involved about K/2 distinct quanta; one flip-flop moves one quantum, and one quantum hopping along a chain is one quantum. Per-branch counting is not defined, but within a branch the causal cone is at least as large as the support, so it would not fall to order one. The readout itself is evidence: the cluster size is seen only because the reversal worked at that breadth.

**So,** under (i)–(iv), N ≳ ~50 (tens, toward 10²), from K ~ 10².

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** The ignorance reading made a posit (a choice of members, defended by the channel basis); the in-body passages assumption named as (iv), the way the bound fails; the count stated by initial site, with MQC size as a stand-in for per-member spread; the bound made conditional; the readout noted as evidence of reversal at that breadth.

**Grade.** Shape. Under (i)–(iv), N ≳ ~50. Owed: the ruling on (iv), whether exchanges inside one body are passages; how much echo returns at K ~ 10² in Sánchez et al. 2020.

## 2026-11-09 — exchanges between patterns inside one body are passages (ruled, candidate)

Ruled on assumption (iv) of the warm-sample entry: energy-conserving exchanges between distinct patterns inside one body (two nuclei's spins swapping a Zeeman quantum by a flip-flop) are passages; kinships are relations within a single pattern (the tiles of one pattern, the quarks of one proton). This settles one half of the open question "whether exchanges inside one pattern are passages or kinships": between patterns, passages; within one pattern, still open. With it, **N ≳ ~50** stands under the remaining named posits and readings (mixture as Zeeman configurations; breadth as support; the pulse trains steer). Cost carried: with passages inside bodies counting, an isolated body whose internal chains pass N would commit, which bears on "a pattern left alone is not resolved" if N is adopted (not adopted; N stays a shape).

## 2026-11-09 — can N be tied to what CO already has? (shape; the ties clash, after cold reading)

**Why.** As a free number, N would be a fourth constant of CO's own. Adopting it is far stronger if it follows from the constants CO already has (δ, P_max, ℓ, ħ). N is a pure number, so the candidates are dimensionless combinations.

**Candidates.**
- *N = P_max/ħ: a chain commits when it has gathered more quanta than one place can hold.* It echoes an existing rule (an empty place commits only over capacity). But capacity is per place and a chain's breadth is spread over many places; no place overflows. Not supported as stated. (P_max itself is no longer pinned to a few ħ: that figure rested on the superseded δ.)
- *N from ℓ alone:* counts built from ℓ (e.g. (λ_C/ℓ)³) depend on the pattern, not a constant.
- *N = 1/δ: a chain commits when the record it has laid down reaches the amount one commitment engages (C4).* If each distinct quantum a chain reaches (ruling 6) left a share δħ, the chain's record would reach ħ at N = 1/δ. A variant with threshold P_max (what one place holds, and what one hop moves) gives N = P_max/(δħ) = (ℓ/ℓ_P)²/4π ≲ 5.1 × 10⁶, with no P_max factor. Since ħ and P_max are both amounts the rules supply, the threshold is a free choice, and any combination δ^a(P_max/ħ)^b would qualify: "no new constant" is weaker than it looks.

**What N = 1/δ would give.** With δ ≳ 1.9 × 10⁻⁷ P_max/ħ (hop bound): 50 ≲ N ≲ 5 × 10⁶ ħ/P_max, a window whose lower end is experimental (conditional on the warm-sample posits) and whose upper end comes from the hop bound. If N = 1/δ and N ≳ 50, then δ ≲ 0.02 and ℓ ≳ 25 ℓ_P √(P_max/ħ), about 4 × 10⁻³⁴ m for P_max = ħ.

**Why it does not hold as a tie.** The record it needs is a new kind of quantity that clashes with standing rules:
- C3's retention is per repeat of a renewing pattern; the passages before N are coherent routes, not repeats, and the engaging pattern's renewals keep δħ anyway. A per-passage or per-quantum record is new, not an extension of C3.
- It would have to be *provisional*: echoes below N reverse, so the share would be erased, against "the record only grows"; tied to the chain's branch, against "amounts are classical"; carrying information about the chain, against the independent-draw posit.
- Light: many passages hand on photons, and light's coefficient rests on "light retains no record".
- `NUCLEUS.md` states "δ is a constant of gravity only"; tying N to δ contradicts it.
(No upper bound on δ on record is broken: the old limits applied to a branched record; the shot noise does not depend on δ; the free stock's end, ∝ 1/δ, is still ≳ 5 × 10³¹ s at δ = 0.02.)

**Conclusion.** N cannot be tied to CO's existing constants without a new, erasable, per-quantum record that clashes with four standing rules and one stated scope. N stays a free number if adopted: a fourth constant, set only by experiment.

**Cold reading, same day; corrected.** Arithmetic confirmed; C3 is per repeat, so the record N = 1/δ needs is new; the provisional record clashes with record-only-grows, classical amounts, the independent draw and light's no-record ruling, and with "δ is a constant of gravity only"; passages and distinct quanta distinguished (ruling 4 rejects counting passages over time); the P_max threshold variant added, and with it the point that the threshold is a free choice; candidate 1's numerical dismissal dropped (P_max unpinned).

**Grade.** Shape. No tie of N to existing constants holds without a new record that clashes with standing rules; N, if adopted, is a fourth constant set by experiment.

N stays a free number if adopted (entry above). A one-page note stating the hypothesis, the signature (a reversal ceiling fixed in breadth while coupling-to-perturbation ratio, size and control fidelity are varied), what counts as a test (spreading by the system's own interactions; breadth measured before reversal), the current bound (N ≳ 50, conditional) and suitable platforms is at `MATTER_notes/N_signature_note.md`, cold-read. ## 2026-11-09 — two electrons: the exchange sign restated, with helium as its consistency check (shape, after cold reading)

**Why.** The smallest case of the gap under the ladder tops (`OPEN_DIRECTIONS_notes/how_patterns_combine.md`): two patterns of one kind, no number change, no loops. This restates the 2026-11-06 shape (unordered pairs, Z₂, capacity not the mechanism, the Berry–Robbins route) and adds the check and what the walk lacks.

**The check.** Helium's spectrum carries the sign in two facts. (1) There is no 1³S level: both electrons in the lowest orbital form a spin singlet. (2) The 1s2s triplet (2³S, 19.82 eV above the ground state) lies below the singlet (2¹S, 20.62 eV), a gap of about 0.80 eV; at first order it is twice an exchange integral K (a model quantity: hydrogen-like orbitals give about 2.4 eV), and the triplet lies lower from spatial antisymmetry with a repulsive interaction (K > 0). Distinguishable electrons would show both 1s2s levels in every spin state, and a 1³S. Spin-½ bosons would have 1³S as the ground state in place of 1¹S, and the singlet below the triplet. Helium is a consistency check on whatever supplies the sign; it does not tell one route to fermions from simply positing antisymmetry.

**The sources, as on 2026-11-06, with corrections.**
- *Capacity:* a hard core forbids double occupancy of a place outright, while spin-½ fermions share a place with opposite spins; it fails before the three-dimensional argument (in 3D hard-core bosons are not fermions; the equivalence is one-dimensional, Girardeau 1960). Not the source.
- *Pauli's route:* compatible, not forced (S1, cold-read).
- *Geometry:* (i) patterns of one kind live on unordered pairs of places: the "one pattern" ruling, not a reading of P1–P8 (P7 frees the relabelling of places, not of possibility). It does not clash with the two-pattern ruling's ψ(x, y): an ordered-pair amplitude with ψ(y, x) = ±ψ(x, y) is a function on unordered pairs; that ruling left the sign open. In three dimensions the unordered pairs allow ±1 (Leinaas–Myrheim 1977). (ii) In Berry and Robbins' construction (Proc. R. Soc. A 453, 1771 (1997); and for solitons, Finkelstein–Rubinstein 1968) the exchange is made equivalent to a 2π turn of the internal state, which would give (−1)^{2S}, −1 for the spinor, under that posit. The construction is debated, not a derivation: Berry and Robbins themselves gave alternative transported bases (J. Phys. A 33, L207 (2000)); see also Allen–Mondragon (quant-ph/0304088) and Papadopoulos–Reyes-Lega (arXiv 0911.0579). The −1 exists in the lattice spinor group (binary octahedral); the walk's covariance under it is owed.

**What the walk lacks.** CO's walk has no transport of the internal state under rotation: hops shift the pattern, nothing turns its spinor frame, and moving each pattern along the swap path leaves both spinors unturned. So (ii) is extra structure, not suggested by the walk. And light is a link field, not a spinor walk, so "transport along the swap" is not defined for it; its bosons come from the capped field's commuting link variables instead.

**Grade.** Silence (the exchange sign), with the 2026-11-06 shape standing: the sign would follow from (i) and (ii), both posits, and (ii) has nothing in the walk to rest on. Helium (no 1³S; 2³S below 2¹S by about 0.80 eV) is the consistency check any source must pass.

## 2026-11-10 — positronium: the first bound state of two of CO's own patterns (candidate, by identification)

**Why positronium.** Binding is the first part of how patterns combine. Hydrogen entered on a fixed classical source, which removes reduced mass, recoil and hyperfine. Helium tests the two-pattern ruling but brings the exchange sign, a silence. An electron and a positron are two elementary patterns of different kinds, so the exchange sign does not enter, and neither is a composite, so the nucleus wall does not enter. The positron is the charge-conjugate walk (the ruling that the generator is odd under C); taking it as a free pattern of opposite charge is assumed here (a pattern, not a hole in a sea; the sea is a shape).

**What the two-pattern ruling gives.** With the which-way field branched and Coulomb acting on the pair amplitude ψ(x, y), the two walks have the two-body Dirac–Coulomb operator as continuum limit (as for helium). The centre of mass and the relative coordinate separate at leading (non-relativistic) order, so the levels are hydrogen's with the reduced mass μ = m/2:

E_n = −μc²α²/(2n²) = −6.80 eV/n²,

and 1S–2S (or 1S–2P) at (3/8)·mc²α²/2 = 5.102 eV, 243.0 nm, 1 233 691 GHz at this order, against 1 233 607 GHz measured (Fee et al. 1993); the 84 GHz difference is order α⁴mc² (α² relative: relativistic, recoil, annihilation and QED terms; the relativistic and recoil parts need the Breit exchange, which the continuum limit drops). A quantum the bound pair absorbs goes into the relative state and its momentum into the centre of mass: the composite answers as one. The same ruling, with the proton taken as one pattern of mass m_p (its structure ignored), lifts hydrogen's external-source conditions on reduced mass and recoil: the Rydberg moves by 1 − μ/m_e = 5.44 × 10⁻⁴, and the hydrogen–deuterium isotope shift follows (Urey 1932). CO supplies the ruling only; the construction is standard.

**Where it stops: the hyperfine split.** Ortho- and parapositronium differ at leading order by (7/12)α⁴mc² = 8.45 × 10⁻⁴ eV, 204.4 GHz (203.39 GHz measured). Of the leading term, 4/7 (116.8 GHz) is the magnetic coupling of the two spins: the bound field's velocity and magnetic parts acting on the pair amplitude, with g = 2 from the vertex shape, in reach of the two-pattern ruling once the bound/emitted split is stated, or as transverse exchange under the capped light field (shape); either way no pattern is made or unmade. The other 3/7 (87.6 GHz) is virtual annihilation, e⁺e⁻ → photon → e⁺e⁻ in the ³S₁ state: number change. So do both lifetimes, parapositronium's 125 ps (two photons) and orthopositronium's 142 ns (three photons). Positronium draws the line inside one atom: the gross binding is reached; what needs patterns made and unmade is not.

**Grade.** Candidate by identification, resting on the two-pattern ruling (candidate) and the free positron (assumed): gross structure −6.80 eV/n², the composite answering as one, reduced mass and recoil for hydrogen. Shape: the magnetic 4/7 of the hyperfine split, owing the bound/emitted split. Silence until number change has a rule: the annihilation 3/7 and both lifetimes.

## 2026-11-10 — number change: the smallest rule that lets a pair become light, priced (shape; proposed, not adopted)

**Question.** Positronium's annihilation part of the hyperfine split and both its lifetimes need a pair to become light. What is the least CO must take on for that, and what does it cost?

**What it takes: two shapes already on record, and nothing else new.**
1. *Matter number free:* the pair-route state space (2026-11-08, shape), second-quantised in the standard way: site modes with canonical brackets, positive Fock metric, vacuum the generator's lowest state.
2. *Light quanta:* the capped link (2026-11-09, shape): flux on each relation bounded by capacity, a quantum link model (Chandrasekharan and Wiese 1997), with Gauss's law per configuration, as the two-pattern ruling already has it.

The vertex is then not a further posit, below the cap (the capped link's raising operator is not unitary, so the hop phase holds only below it); the Born identification for varying number is still owed. The hop phase e^{iqA} (ruleds, candidate), with A a link operator, steps the link's flux by one unit at each charged hop: a quantum emitted or absorbed. With matter second-quantised, the same hop mixes the walk's two bands where the field varies (2026-11-09 vertex entry): a pair made or unmade. So e⁺e⁻ → light is one rung of hops on a quantum link, as in Kogut–Susskind lattice QED with the link capped.

**What comes with it.**
- *The exchange sign.* With the generator's branch fixed by the C ruling (2026-11-06), the standard quantisation in item 1 admits only anticommutators (Pauli 1940; the standing wording on statistics). Adopting item 1 would select −1 for CO's patterns by Pauli's argument on its inputs, in the sector where the band does not meet ±π; it does not derive it, and "Statistics open" stands until item 1 is adopted. The two silences would be answered by one bundle of adopted inputs; CO derives neither. In the doubler sector, where the band meets ±π, the C ruling fails and so does the selection.
- *Light's statistics:* commuting link variables (a choice, not a result), so quanta of light are bosons.
- *Selection rule.* Each quantum of light is odd under the swap of the carried sign (the which-way phase reverses under C). Parapositronium (C even) then decays to an even number of quanta and orthopositronium (C odd) to an odd number, three at least (one is forbidden by energy and momentum, not by C); the extra power of α and the three-body phase space are why ortho lives longer, by 9π/[4(π² − 9)α] ≈ 1114 at leading order.
- *By identification at tree level:* τ(para) = 2ħ/(α⁵mc²) = 124.5 ps (125.2 ps measured), τ(ortho)⁻¹ = [2(π² − 9)/(9π)]·α⁶mc²/ħ, giving 138.7 ns (142.0 ns measured; the difference is order α/π), and the annihilation 3/7 of the hyperfine split. Dirac computed free-pair annihilation in 1930; the bound-state lifetimes (Pirenne 1946–47, Ore and Powell 1949) lie past the scope line.

**What it costs.**
- *Loops, and with them the frame.* Once pairs can be made and unmade, virtual pairs dress every pattern. On a lattice with a frame, these give species-dependent speeds at order α/π (by analogy from Collins et al. 2004), against electron–photon bounds near 10⁻¹⁵: a tuning of about 10¹⁷ or exclusion. Tree level escapes the O(α/π) term, not the Wilson walk's own tree-level bending (2026-11-08 scope); the posit does not stop at tree level. This is the frame problem arriving through this door, as `OPEN_DIRECTIONS_notes/how_patterns_combine.md` expected.
- *Light's speed.* A capped link gives a photon only in a phase that supports one; in three dimensions such a phase exists (the emergent photon of quantum spin ice: Hermele, Fisher and Balents, PRB 69, 064404 (2004)), but its speed is set by the link dynamics. The ruling that light moves at the walk's c then becomes a condition on that dynamics, satisfied by tuning or by something not yet found.
- *Hamiltonian against step.* Quantum link models are written with a Hamiltonian; CO's walk is a step. A stepped circuit of local gates for the link field (Floquet lattice gauge theory) is owed.
- *The three adopted inputs* of item 1 (canonical brackets, positive metric, lowest vacuum) are standard field theory; the primitives supply none of them (standing wording, 2026-11-06).

**Grade.** Shape proposed, not adopted. Taken, it would give positronium's annihilation part and lifetimes by identification, light as bosons, the selection rule, and the exchange sign as −1 outside the doubler sector, at the price of making loops live, which brings the frame's O(α/π) exposure from risk to the next thing to face.

## 2026-11-10 — number change held (the author's choice); the bound/emitted split at order v², and the magnetic part of the hyperfine split

**Held.** The number-change shape above is held, not adopted, as quantised light was. Statistics stays open; CO's scope stays without number change.

**The split, stated at order v² (candidate; a posit extending the two-pattern ruling, by identification).** The two-pattern ruling makes what a relation carries branched with the configuration of what passes, because what sets it has amplitude. The Coulomb part was read so. The magnetic part is set by the patterns' currents and spins, which have amplitude. That alone does not separate bound from emitted, since currents with amplitude also source radiation: Coulomb is bound by a constraint (Gauss's law), while the magnetostatic field is a dynamical field eliminated at order v². So the split is a posit, not a reading. Stated:

*The part of the which-way field fixed by the positions, currents and spins of what passes, at the same moment (the Coulomb field and, at order v², the magnetostatic field of the currents and spins), is bound: it acts on the pair amplitude, each pattern's own part counted in its mass. The part that has left its sources is emitted: light.*

This extends the self-parts line (gravity record, 2026-11-08) to the cross terms. With it, the two-body operator gains the Breit interaction at order v² (Breit 1929), which the bare Dirac–Coulomb continuum limit drops. (The term named retardation is part of it in Coulomb gauge; frequency dependence enters at higher order and belongs with the emitted part.) Coulomb plus Breit is frame-independent to order v²/c² (Darwin); the simultaneity cost on record remains beyond it.

**Positronium's magnetic part.** For S states only the contact term of the spin–spin coupling survives. Each spin carries μ = ±(g/2)(e/m)S with g = 2 (the vertex rulings, candidate; tree level), and

H_ss = (2μ₀/3)(e²/m²) S₁·S₂ δ³(r),

with |ψ(0)|² = 1/(πa³), a = 2a₀ for the reduced mass m/2, and S₁·S₂ differing by ħ² between triplet and singlet:

ΔE_mag = (2μ₀/3)(e²ħ²/m²)/(8πa₀³) = α⁴mc²/3 = 4.83 × 10⁻⁴ eV, 116.8 GHz,

which is the magnetic 4/7 of the leading 204.4 GHz. The other 3/7 is annihilation, held with number change.

**Hydrogen's 21 cm line, with the proton taken as one pattern.** The same contact term with the proton's moment g_p = 5.586 (imported; the nucleus wall) gives

ΔE = (4/3) g_p (m_e/m_p) α⁴m_ec² (1 + m_e/m_p)⁻³ ≈ 1418.8 MHz,

against 1420.406 MHz measured. The 0.11% gap is mainly the electron's anomalous moment (a_e ≈ 0.00116, which needs loops, held): with it, 1420.5 MHz; the remaining −50 ppm is relativistic, QED and proton-structure (Zemach).

**Grade.** The split at order v²: candidate, a posit extending the two-pattern ruling, by identification; proposed for confirmation, since it fixes where light begins. The magnetic hyperfine parts: candidate by identification, resting on it (positronium 116.8 GHz; hydrogen 1418.8 MHz at tree level, with g_p imported). Unchanged: the annihilation part, the lifetimes and the anomalous moment (silence; number change and loops held).

## 2026-11-10 — the frame with loops, priced: the exits and what each costs (after cold reading)

**The problem, as on record.** On a lattice that picks a frame, loops give each species its own top speed at order α/π ≈ 2 × 10⁻³ (Collins et al. 2004, by analogy for QED), against bounds near 5 × 10⁻²⁰: about 1 part in 10¹⁷. With number change held, CO has no loops, but nature does (the electron's anomalous moment is measured), so the problem is deferred, not avoided. The question here is what would remove it, and whether any exit is CO-shaped.

**The exits.**
1. *A substrate that picks no frame.* Random sprinkling does (Bombelli–Henson–Sorkin 2009), and is excluded for CO (births, degree, distance, locality). A regular lattice can carry integral Lorentz transformations as a point set (Schild 1948), but any finite-degree graph on it picks a frame (the finite-group argument, 2026-11-09). Way B (discreteness of values, not places) is parked. *Not available.*
2. *Flow to Lorentz invariance at low energy.* In gauge theories, species' speeds are drawn together as energy falls (Chadha and Nielsen 1983; Anber and Donoghue 2011). For QED the pull is only logarithmic, as a power of the running coupling, η ∝ [α(μ)/α(μ*)]^{2b/5} with b of order one: from the hop scale ħc/ℓ ≈ 1.5 × 10¹⁵ GeV down to 1 GeV it shrinks a difference by a factor of order one, not 10¹⁷. Only a coupling that stays strong gives power-law flow, but the photon–electron pair is fixed by QED. *Excluded as sufficient.*
3. *Keep the frame out of matter's sector.* If the frame lived only in gravity, matter's loops would see it only through gravitational coupling, suppressed by (M_LV/M_Pl)² (Pospelov and Shang 2012). In CO matter walks on the substrate that carries the frame. *Not available.*
4. *A protecting symmetry pairing walk patterns with light quanta.* Supersymmetry forbids the dimension-four terms; they return only through its breaking, suppressed by (M_SUSY/M)² (Groot Nibbelink and Pospelov 2005), harmless for any breaking scale below about 10⁷ GeV against a 1.5 × 10¹⁵ GeV hop scale (from (M_SUSY/M)²·α/π < 5 × 10⁻²⁰). CO has fermion walks and boson links, the right two kinds, but no partners: each pattern would need a link-like partner and each light quantum a walk-like one, none of which is seen. One walk for all species (the author's faces picture) does not protect, since electron and neutrino couple to light differently, so their loops differ. *Shape, not CO's.*
5. *Tuning.* The bare speeds in the walk and link are set so that the renormalised speeds agree to 1 part in 10¹⁷, species by species. "Light at the walk's c" is already this kind of statement at tree level; with loops it becomes a condition on renormalised speeds, as anisotropic lattice QCD tunes its bare anisotropy. It is consistent, costs no new structure, and explains nothing; a fine-tuned parameter, like the cosmological constant, though far milder. *Permitted, at a tuning of about 10¹⁷.*

**Where this leaves CO.** Not excluded: permitted with a tuning, the same position as any real-time lattice field theory. The CO-shaped way out, if one exists, would be a real-time discrete structure with a symmetry that ties species' speeds together without a frame-free substrate; none is known, and finding one would be new in its own right.

**Grade.** Silence (Lorentz invariance once loops come in), now priced: exits 1 and 3 not available, 2 excluded as sufficient, 4 a shape outside CO, 5 permitted with a tuning of about 10¹⁷.

## 2026-11-10 — engagement versus route, read with today's pieces (a reading; no ruling)

What the free-share rule, the channel width and "record is not possibility" add to the owed posit (which passages are engagements).

- *The continuum clause becomes a consequence.* With a channel read as a final state of width Γ (ruled, candidate), the golden rule gives Γ = (2π/ħ)|V|²ρ_f, ρ_f the density of final states. A discrete final state (one cavity mode) has no width: passage through it stays coherent even on resonance (vacuum Rabi oscillation), and a rate exists only where final states form a continuum. C8 stated that separately; it now follows from the width.
- *Width and breadth are one chain seen two ways.* A final state's width is how fast its onward passages spread; the stopping number N is how far they must spread for the engagement to be a commitment. On that reading a chain reaches breadth N after a time of order N/Γ for steady spreading (shape; the growth law of breadth is not read), so N carries a time signature as well as a size one.
- *Regular clock, chance engagement: no conflict.* The free-share rule acts on free possibility, which has no amplitude, so the clock is regular. Engagement acts on possibility in transit, which has amplitude, so its outcome is a draw from |a|². Chance enters exactly where amplitude does.
- *What stays owed.* N's value, the growth law of breadth, and a test (reversal failing at a fixed breadth, the N signature) are as before. The new pieces narrow the posit (no separate continuum clause) but do not supply it.

Proposed for the results docs (not applied): in MATTER's C8 clause, "a rate only where its final states form a continuum" marked as following from the width.

## 2026-11-10 — correction to the previous entry's first bullet, and how breadth grows (after cold reading; shape)

**Correction.** The previous entry used Γ = (2π/ħ)|V|²ρ_f for the channel's width; that is the decay rate of the initial state into a continuum, not the width of the final state, and the golden rule does not give a discrete level "zero width", it does not apply to one. Corrected: with the width ruling, a final state of width Γ (from its own onward loss) gives the engagement rate

R = 4|V|²/(ħ²Γ) for |V| ≪ ħΓ (weak coupling, irreversible), while for |V| ≫ ħΓ passage stays coherent even on resonance (vacuum Rabi oscillation, a cavity mode broad but slow against the coupling).

So the continuum clause follows as "a rate only where the final state is broad against the coupling" (MATTER updated). The point stands with the corrected criterion: whether passage can be an engagement is set by the final state's width against the coupling.

**How breadth grows.** The previous entry's t_N ~ N/Γ assumed steady, linear spreading: one channel leaking quanta at a rate Γ, B(t) ≈ Γt, so t_N ≈ N/Γ. Real engagers are cascades: an amplifier stage, a phonon splitting into two, a spin cluster in a three-dimensional solid. With gain g per stage of time τ,

B(t) = g^{t/τ},  t_N = τ ln N / ln g.

For a cascade the time to commit grows only as ln N: N ≈ 50 and N ≈ 10⁶ differ by a factor of about 3.5 in time. So timing is a weak signature of N in any detector and the strong one stays size: reversal failing at a fixed breadth. Which law a given engager follows is set by its onward channels; neither is ruled.

## 2026-11-11 — G1's method turned on the frame problem (reading; no run needed)

**The idea.** Run G1 counted the costs that the cubic group plus a gauge freedom allow for gravity's carriers and found them isotropic. Could the same count show that the walk's symmetries (cubic group, C, the palindromic order, time reversal) forbid species-dependent top speeds at one loop?

**Why the outcome is already known.** The term loops generate is c_μν ψ̄γ^μ∂^ν ψ with c_μν ∝ u_μu_ν, u the lattice's rest frame (Collins et al. 2004): an isotropic rescaling of a species' time against its space, its speed. It is invariant under every rotation, so under the cubic group too, and under C, P and T. G1 itself shows the same thing on the gravity side: its two-dimensional space was a strength and a speed, and the gauge freedom did not fix the speed. So the count would return one allowed speed term per species, as it returned one for gravity. No symmetry the walk has forbids it, and the protocol does not call for a run.

**What it adds.** (1) The frame problem has the same structure in every sector, gravity included: each sector carries a speed that symmetry leaves free. At tree level CO sets them equal by giving every sector the same tick (one hop per tick, ℓ/c), and the walk's Wilson angle sets matter's massless speed c₀ = 2cos w, so equality at tree level also needs one w for all species. Loops renormalise each sector's speed separately. (2) GW170817, gravity's speed equal to light's to ~10⁻¹⁵, is a bound in the same family as the Crab bounds on matter: CO must keep gravity's speed and light's together through loops too. (3) What would protect equality is a symmetry relating sectors' speeds; in the continuum that is Lorentz boosts. Universal coupling to one metric (every walk reading the same local frame, P7c) makes speeds equal at tree level, not under loops.

**Grade.** Reading. The frame problem stands; its statement now includes gravity's speed, bounded by GW170817.

## 2026-11-11 — the exchange sign: three dimensions allow only ±1 (reading); which sign, by the frames' turn (shape)

**What the rules say.** Two patterns of one kind live on unordered pairs of places: nothing labels which is which. P8 adds the amplitudes of routes that reach the same configuration, and the direct route and the exchanged route reach the same one, with a relative factor the rules have not fixed (the silence, as stated in OPEN_DIRECTIONS).

**Part 1: only ±1 (needed, given P8 and three dimensions).** The relative factor is the amplitude picked up by a closed loop in the space of unordered pairs that swaps the two. For amplitudes that compose along routes (P8), that factor is a representation of the fundamental group of that space (with the two not allowed at one place). In three or more dimensions the fundamental group of unordered pairs is Z₂ (Leinaas and Myrheim 1977): doing the swap twice is a loop that can be shrunk away, so the factor squared is 1, and the factor is +1 or −1. In two dimensions the group is the braid group and any phase is allowed (anyons). So three dimensions, which CO reaches as a consistency condition, also limit the exchange to ±1. This narrows the silence from "any relative factor" to a two-way choice. Two cautions (cold reading). On the cubic graph the space of pairs is itself a graph, whose loops form a free group: every elementary square is an independent loop that could carry a phase, and the which-way phase (P7d) is such a link phase. Z₂ returns only if the exchange factor is flat: no phase around elementary squares, including the "A moves then B / B moves then A" squares, once the which-way phase is removed; and the excluded set (two patterns meeting on an edge as they swap) needs defining. For three or more patterns the group is the permutation group, whose higher representations allow parastatistics; Part 1 does not exclude it.

**Part 2: which sign (shape).** The walk's internal state is the Clifford four, which turns as a spinor: a full turn of space multiplies it by −1. Each place carries a local frame (P7c), and moving a pattern along a route transports its internal state with those frames. The swap loop of two patterns can be deformed into one pattern's frame turning a full turn relative to the other's (the belt trick; Finkelstein and Rubinstein 1968 for extended objects; Berry and Robbins 1997 for a construction in ordinary quantum mechanics). If the exchange factor is the factor the internal states pick up along that transport, it is (−1)^{2s}: −1 for the walk's spin-½ patterns, +1 for integer spin. That is the spin–statistics connection, and it splits the cases the record asks for: electrons and helium-3 (odd numbers of spin-½ constituents) exclude; helium-4 and rubidium-87 (even numbers) and photons merge.

**What it rests on.** Part 1: P8, unordered configurations with no coincidence, three dimensions, and a flat exchange factor. Part 2 also assumes the nucleons are walk patterns and that the photon's internal state turns with integer spin; no non-relativistic spin–statistics theorem exists. (Composite counts: helium-4 has 6 spin-½ constituents, helium-3 has 5, rubidium-87 has 124; (−1)^{2s} for the whole and (−1)^N over constituents agree, since orbital motion adds whole units.) Part 2: that the exchange factor is the frame transport's, an identification; the Berry–Robbins construction is debated in the literature, and on the cubic graph rotations are discrete, so "a full turn" is the spinor's 2π read through the frames. Not covered: what two patterns do on one stock beyond the sign (the two-pattern dynamics), and second quantisation.

**Grade.** Part 1: needed, given P8, three dimensions, no coincidence and a flat exchange factor (a posit on the graph: no phase around elementary squares once the which-way phase is removed); for three or more patterns parastatistics is not excluded. Part 2: shape, a candidate if the identification is ruled (the exchange factor is the frames' transport).

**Ruled (2026-11-11, candidate).** (a) The exchange factor is flat on the graph. (b) Its value is the factor the internal states pick up as the places' frames carry them through the swap. Entered: appendix P8b, MATTER, OPEN_DIRECTIONS, the plain doc, README.

## 2026-11-11 — the frame problem restated with gravity in view: no backdrop held exactly would protect the speeds, but cannot hold exactly for interacting matter on a lattice (reading)

**Why restate.** Two things now rest on the frame: matter's loops (species speeds at order α/π against bounds near 10⁻²⁰) and gravity's nonlinear rule (its uniqueness assumes Lorentz-invariant interactions). Ruled: no backdrop at every order, and runs G3 and G4 found it exact on the lattice at linear order for the carriers and for free matter. This entry asks what the ruling says about the frame.

**1. In the long-wavelength theory the ruling excludes a frame.** Every frame-picking term of dimension ≤ 4 (the Standard-Model Extension's a_μ, b_μ, c_μν, d_μν, the photon's k_F, …) is a fixed background tensor contracted with the fields. A fixed tensor that does not move when places are moved and clocks reset is a backdrop; with no backdrop at every order it is excluded unless it is itself a transforming field (an Einstein-aether-type carrier, which CO does not have). So a frame can come only from the lattice, through the regulator failing to keep no backdrop exactly.

**2. Exact no backdrop on the lattice, for interacting matter, would conserve the total energy current.** On an infinite lattice with fields vanishing far away, the G4 identities, if they held for the full interacting theory, give: summing identity II over links, the flux differences cancel and P = Σ J is exactly conserved; summing identity I by parts, dX_E/dt = P with X_E = Σ_x x E_x. So the centre of energy moves uniformly, and K = tP − X_E is conserved. P is not the generator of lattice translations (crystal momentum); its conservation is a separate demand.

**3. That would force equal speeds.** At low energy each species' energy current is J_s = c_s² p_s (Ev = ½∇E² = c²p for a dimension-four dispersion; with anisotropic speeds a matrix C_s, and the same argument applies). Interactions conserve Σ p_s. If Σ c_s² p_s were also conserved, any process moving momentum between species (Compton scattering, electron and photon) would force equal c_s at dimension four. So no backdrop held exactly would be the protecting symmetry the frame note calls unknown, for the regulator-generated speeds.

**4. But it cannot hold exactly for interacting matter on a lattice.** On a lattice the dispersion is periodic, so J(p) = ½∇E²(p) is not linear in p beyond long wavelength. For elastic 2 → 2 scattering in 3 + 1 the final state has six unknowns; energy, crystal momentum and Σ J impose seven conditions, so exact conservation of Σ J forces trivial scattering at generic momenta. Equivalently, the additive invariants of elastic collisions with a smooth dispersion are combinations of 1, p and E (Boltzmann's collision-invariant theorem); J would have to be one, which for a periodic nonconstant E is impossible. A check (`MATTER_runs/frame_energy_current/j_scattering_check.py`, Wilson–Dirac dispersion, m = 0.3, r = 1): in elastic 2 → 2 events on the energy and crystal-momentum shell, the total J changes by a median 1.6 × 10⁻³, 2.1 × 10⁻², 1.8 × 10⁻¹ of itself at momentum scales 0.05, 0.2, 0.6 (hop units), growing as (kℓ)². Interacting 1 + 1 integrable models (the XXZ chain, whose total energy current is conserved: Zotos, Naef and Prelovšek 1997) escape only because 2 → 2 scattering in one dimension is trivial.

**So.** In the long-wavelength theory, CO's ruling excludes frame-picking terms. On the lattice, for interacting matter, no backdrop can hold only up to order (kℓ)²: the regulator breaks it there, and loops can multiply that by the regulator's power divergence, so species speeds can split at order α/π as before. The frame problem is unchanged in size. What is new: it is now located exactly (the lattice's failure to conserve the energy current under scattering), the "exact no backdrop" exit is closed for interacting matter, and gravity's nonlinear rule shares the same assumption.

**Not covered.** Discrete time (the walk's tick); whether a cancellation between species (the walk and the links) could keep the induced speeds equal without exact conservation; non-generic kinematics; 1 + 1 integrable escapes.

**Grade.** Reading. Steps 1–3: given the ruling and the G4 identities. Step 4: the exact-no-backdrop exit for interacting matter on a lattice is excluded by kinematics (collision invariants), with a numerical check. The frame problem stands as before; tuning remains the only exit known.

## 2026-11-11 — the walk–link cancellation exit, read against lattice practice (reading)

**The question.** With exact no backdrop closed for interacting matter (entry above), could the walk (matter) and the links (light) break it in matching ways, so that their induced speeds shift together without any exact conservation?

**The nearest known case.** Two analogues. Anisotropic lattice gauge theory (Euclidean, with a time step different from the space step) is the best studied; Hamiltonian lattice gauge theory (Kogut–Susskind: continuous real time, discrete space) is the closer one to CO, and there too the speed of light is renormalised and must be fixed. In both, the hypercubic symmetry that mixes time with space is gone, leaving the spatial cubic group. There, the renormalised anisotropy (the effective speed of light) differs from the bare one, and it differs between the gauge sector and the fermion sector: the bare speed in the fermion action and the bare anisotropy of the gauge action must be tuned separately so that both sectors see the same renormalised light cone (Klassen 1998 for the gauge action; dynamical tuning by Morrin, Ó Cais, Peardon, Ryan and Skullerud 2006, where quark and gluon parameters are tuned simultaneously because each feeds the other; discussed in Fodor and Hoelbling 2012, §II). On the isotropic Euclidean lattice the speed of light is not renormalised, because the operators that could shift it are forbidden by the hypercubic group; that protection is exactly what a real-time network lacks.

**Reading.** The one known automatic mechanism, the flow of species' speeds toward a common value at long wavelength (Chadha–Nielsen 1983), is logarithmic and far too slow for the bounds (frame note). Beyond it, no walk–link cancellation is known; in the studied lattice theories without a symmetry mixing time and space, the two sectors' speeds renormalise differently and are matched by tuning. CO's walk and links have different hop rules (the walk's palindromic Wilson step; the links' own update), so there is no structural reason for their induced shifts to agree.

**Grade.** The cancellation exit: not excluded in principle, but unsupported; lattice practice offers no instance. With this, every exit in the frame note is read: tuning remains the only known one, and a real-time symmetry that mixes the tick with the hops (unknown) the only unexplored one.

## 2026-11-11 — the N test made concrete, and a weakness in it found (reading)

The N note's test needs a dial on Σ/J under a qualifying Hamiltonian. Sánchez et al. (arXiv:2112.00607) have one: the secular dipolar Hamiltonian of adamantane scaled by k ≈ 0.05–0.9 by off-resonance irradiation, with the unreversed part reported roughly constant, so Σ/J is nominally ∝ 1/k. They did not report the breadth K* at echo loss against k; the note now proposes adding spin counting (about an axis perpendicular to the scaled Hamiltonian's effective-field axis) at a fixed echo fraction.

**The weakness (found by cold reading).** In the perturbation-independent regime that group reports (T₃ ∝ T₂), standard quantum mechanics also gives a K* flat in k: the echo's time and the cluster's growth both scale with kJ. So the note's original table, "K* keeps growing as Σ/J falls" against "K* stops at N", holds only below the crossover where decay depends on Σ again, which shrinks with system size (Zangara et al.). In large systems emergent irreversibility gives a ceiling too. The discriminant is now two-fold: K* below the crossover (QM: grows), and the ceiling's universality across crystals, Hamiltonians and platforms (QM: set by the dynamics, shifts; CO: the same N). The note is rewritten accordingly. The contrast of about 10² spins (secular) against about 10⁴ (double-quantum, earlier works) is recorded as the pattern CO would show if N were near 10², and as not evidence.

**Grade.** Reading. The N test is sharper and also weaker than stated before: a single-system ceiling no longer counts; universality does. N ≳ 50 stands.

## 2026-11-11 — a second platform for the N universality check: none yet reports a qualifying ceiling (literature read)

Read for a qualifying echo with breadth counted in quanta outside adamantane. Rydberg gases (Geier et al. 2024) reverse a flip-flop (XX) Hamiltonian by state transfer, which qualifies, but count no cluster size; their main unreversed part is atomic motion. Fluorapatite spin counting uses the double-quantum Hamiltonian (does not qualify). Superconducting-qubit lattices with natural hopping qualify but reach a few quanta. So the universality row cannot yet be filled; the Rydberg platform is the natural second, given spin counting at echo loss. N ≳ 50 stands. Entered in the N note.

The shape (a renewing pattern with no channel, made only at the start) was read against today's rulings. Four consequences, none asked for when the shape was written, each scoped after cold reading: (1) universal gravitational coupling and the equivalence principle at linear order, from the single coupling forced by no backdrop for the walk (candidate); (2) no production by new places (void births are possibility-side); other routes (gravitational pair production, production through gravity) are unruled, since CO has no number-change rule; (3) free of the frame problem's α/π loop split, with the lattice's tree-level mass-dependent excess and uncomputed gravitational loops remaining, against loose dark-sector bounds; (4) given CO's present inventory (every elementary pattern a spin-½ walk pattern; no non-gravitational binding in the strict shape; no channel-less link field), fermionic, so the Pauli phase-space bound from dwarf spheroidals applies, m ≥ 0.13 keV at 95% (Alvey et al. 2021), and ultralight bosonic dark matter is excluded. A dark–dark channel would be an addition (the superfluid doodle now says so). Entered in the dark-matter note and OPEN_DIRECTIONS. Grade: readings of the shape; the shape stays a shape.

## 2026-11-11 — the photon ratio η against the beginning's epoch (reading; sketch with stated assumptions)

Read the matter–antimatter hunch (a saturated place makes one matter pattern, no room for a second) against the beginning's epoch (every place over capacity until N_exit = N₀ + E₀/(P_max − P_b)). Assumptions, none ruled: the excess ends as light; a quantum per tick carries ħc/ℓ; heat balance on the network, then adiabatic cooling with no large later entropy production; the beginning's matter count survives a bath at or above the cutoff. Then kT ≈ (P_max/ħ)(ħc/ℓ)/2 (equipartition, two modes per place), at or above the cutoff; entropy per place S ≈ 2[ln(P_max/ħ) + 1], becoming about S/3.6 photons per place after cooling (1–10 for P_max/ħ up to 10⁴; ~50 for 10⁴⁰). (1) The literal hunch, one matter pattern for every saturated place, gives η ~ 0.02–1: excluded, given the assumptions, by seven to nine orders. (2) Matter only from the first N₀ places, light over all N_exit: η ≈ (N₀/N_exit)(0.1–1) ≈ N₀(P_max − P_b)/E₀ × (0.1–1), so N_exit/N₀ ≈ 10⁸–10⁹ (≈18–21 e-folds in places, ≈6–7 in length): a shape that ties η to the first excess; missing a rule for which places make matter; short of standard inflation's ~60 e-folds (size comparison only), whose need in CO is open. Entered in the dark-matter note (after cold reading, which corrected the thermal estimate from a continuum T⁴ law to equipartition above the cutoff and listed the assumptions).

## 2026-11-11 — what makes the first places different: a candidate for the ruling (proposed)

**The gap.** The first-places version of the photon ratio (η ≈ N₀(P_max − P_b)/E₀ × (0.1–1)) needs only the first N₀ places to make matter, while the later over-capacity places of the beginning's epoch also commit.

**What the rules already give.** (1) C7: the first commitments come out of the beginning, unordered. (2) Number change is held (priced, not added): no rule makes new patterns after they exist, and none destroys them, so the matter count, once set, is kept through the epoch; that also supplies the η sketch's assumption that the beginning's matter survives the hot bath, as long as number change stays held. (3) A pattern is what renews; a commitment that does not repeat is a single event that leaves record.

**What is missing.** Nothing says whether a commitment forced by over-capacity in a place born during the epoch starts a renewing pattern or is a single event.

**The candidate (proposed for the ruling).** *Only the first commitments, out of the beginning, start renewing patterns. A commitment forced later by over-capacity is a single event: it turns possibility into record and releases the rest in transit, and does not renew.* In the author's terms: matter is what the One Being turned definite as; what the spreading forces afterwards is happening, not new matter.

**What it would give.** With the hunch (one pattern per first place, no room for a second, one sign from one event): N₀ matter patterns, all of one sign, kept by the held number change; the epoch's later commitments add record and light only; then η ≈ N₀(P_max − P_b)/E₀ × (0.1–1), given the η sketch's other assumptions. It also gives dark patterns the same origin (made only at the start), as the dark-matter shape already requires.

**What it costs and risks.** A distinction between commitments by origin (first vs forced), a posit. It must not contradict: C3 and C4 (renewal conditions, which do not refer to origin: the candidate makes origin, not local conditions, decide whether a renewal chain starts); the first-epoch record entries (single events add record; consistent). If number change is later added, matter made in pairs from light in the hot era must leave the net count as the first commitments set it (the "net excess" reading in the dark-matter note).

**Grade.** Proposed, shape. It would turn η into a relation with the beginning's first excess, not derive its value.

**Ruled (2026-11-11, candidate).** Only the first commitments start renewing patterns; a commitment forced later in the epoch by over-capacity is a single event. Entered: appendix C7; plain doc C7; dark-matter note; OPEN_DIRECTIONS (dark matter and matter over antimatter). Consequence for C1 (found by cold reading): C1 and the Renewal ruling (space and gravity record, "any commitment does this if free possibility is adjacent") said every commitment renews when fed; C7's ruling makes a forced commitment in the epoch not renew though fed. C1 now reads "a commitment of a pattern (a first commitment or one of its repeats)", and points to C7. Outside the beginning's epoch nothing changes: every commitment there is a pattern's.

Read: (1) the floor is held, not falling (newborn places bring their share), and a uniform floor makes no force anyway; (2) bound regions add no places, and void births come in place and make no field, so births do not disturb a galaxy's field; (3) with the nonlinear cost Einstein–Hilbert plus Λ (given its premises), expansion acts on bound systems only as in GR, at accelerations of order H²r, about 4 × 10⁻⁵ of a₀ at 30 kpc (H²r/a₀ = 2πHr/c); a MOND-like change needs a nonlocal term (excluded by locality) or an extra field (absent from CO's present inventory).
