## 2026-11-11 — the beginning's numbers, read against data; a correction to the literal-hunch exclusion; number change needed (reading)

**Setup.** With C7 as ruled (only the first commitments start renewing patterns) and the η sketch's assumptions, the beginning has three numbers: N₀ first places, the first excess E₀, and the capacity P_max (P_b known in shape, about (d − 1)ħ).

**1. N₀ from data.** Under C7, every persisting matter pattern is a first commitment: in the observable universe about 10⁸⁰ protons and neutrons, as many electrons, and dark patterns in a number set by their mass. So N₀ ~ 10⁸⁰ per observable volume, known to a factor of a few. C7's "few places" must be read as few against today's places, (R/ℓ)³ ≈ 4 × 10¹⁷² for the observable universe's radius R ≈ 4.4 × 10²⁶ m and ℓ = 1.3 × 10⁻³¹ m.

**2. N_exit and the exit temperature.** With one to fifty photons per place after heat balance, N_exit ≈ N_γ/(1–50) ≈ 10^87.5–10^89, N_γ ≈ 1.5 × 10⁸⁹. The η sketch's equipartition gives kT_exit ≈ (P_max/2ħ)(ħc/ℓ); one photon's worth per place corresponds to kT_exit near ħc/ℓ ≈ 1.5 × 10¹⁵ GeV × (1.3 × 10⁻³¹ m/ℓ). Places and photons both scale as a³ under adiabatic cooling, so the count of places today follows by construction, not as a check.

**3. E₀ in units of the room per place.** E₀ ≈ N_exit (P_max − P_b) ≈ 10^87.5–10^89 (P_max − P_b). Given P_b, one free scale is left, P_max, which also enters N_exit weakly (through ln P_max). The photon ratio is not predicted; data fix the beginning's numbers up to P_max.

**4. Correction: the literal hunch is not excluded.** The η sketch compared all matter patterns per photon with η, but η counts baryons. If every saturated place makes one pattern, there are 0.02–1 patterns per photon; relic neutrinos plus baryons are about 0.8 per photon (336 and 0.0000006 × 411, against 411 cm⁻³). So the literal hunch fits if almost all first patterns are neutrinos, about one in 10⁹ a baryon. It then needs a rule for which kind a place makes (a silence), and it gives the relic neutrinos one sign. C7 as ruled (only the first places make patterns) and the literal hunch are now two readings; the C7 ruling was entered partly on the exclusion now withdrawn, noted.

**5. Number change is needed.** Pair creation and annihilation are observed in the laboratory every day (positron sources; γγ → e⁺e⁻), and standard cosmology needs a thermal electron–positron population (nucleosynthesis; the relic-neutrino temperature T_ν/T_γ = (4/11)^{1/3} records its annihilation). So number change cannot stay held: it is needed, in a form that conserves net matter minus antimatter (baryon and lepton number; kinds change in weak decays, and neutrino flavours oscillate). The relic neutrinos then can come from pairs in the hot era, as in standard physics, under C7 as ruled; under the literal hunch they would be first patterns. Its rule is not written; adding it brings the loop exposure the frame problem prices.

**Grade.** Reading. N₀ ~ 10⁸⁰ and E₀ in units of P_max − P_b: readings given C7 and the η sketch. The literal-hunch exclusion: withdrawn. Number change: needed (laboratory pair creation; the hot era), rule not written.

**Kept (2026-11-11).** C7 as ruled is kept over the literal hunch, after the correction above: it needs no rule for kinds, and the relic neutrinos come from pairs in the hot era, as in standard physics.

## 2026-11-11 — the number-change rule: a proposal (for the ruling)

**What it must do.** Make and end pairs (laboratory; the hot era), conserving energy, momentum and charge, and net matter minus antimatter (baryon and lepton number), so the first commitments set only the net excess.

**A clash it must settle first.** C7 as ruled says only the first commitments start renewing patterns. A pair made from light starts two. So C7 needs one clause, or number change cannot exist: *the net count of patterns is set by the first commitments; afterwards patterns start only in pairs of opposite sign, and a commitment forced by over-capacity starts none.*

**The smallest rule, in CO's terms.** The walk already has negative-energy states (appendix §3: Dirac's Hamiltonian). Read the walk as a field rather than one pattern at a time, with the negative-energy states taken as filled; an antipattern is then a missing negative-energy state, of opposite charge and opposite count. The which-way coupling, already ruled (a U(1) phase acting on amplitudes), then moves a quantum from a filled negative state to a positive one when light supplies at least 2mc²: a pair is made; the reverse ends one and releases light. Nothing new is posited for the coupling; what is posited is the reading of the walk as a field with its negative states filled (or, equivalently, second quantisation of the walk, which with spin ½ admits only anticommutators: Pauli 1940). In standard terms this is lattice quantum electrodynamics on the walk.

**What it gives.** Pair making and annihilation with the walk's own coupling (g = 2 already); the electron–positron bath of the hot era; relic neutrinos (through the weak coupling, which CO has not built: a further gap); net charge and net count conserved automatically, since a pair has zero of each.

**What it costs.** (1) The filled negative states carry a vacuum energy, as in any field theory; in CO it would have to be either not a source of gravity or cancelled, which is the cosmological-constant problem, unsolved here as everywhere. (2) Loops: with pairs, the walk's self-energy shifts each species' speed at order α/π, which is the frame problem (already priced). (3) The weak interaction, needed for neutrinos and for baryon/lepton kinds to change, is not in CO.

**Grade.** Proposed: the C7 clause (needs a ruling); the walk read as a field with filled negative states (an identification with lattice QED, candidate if ruled). Its consequences beyond what QED already gives: none new.

**Ruled (2026-11-11, candidate).** Both parts of the proposal above: the C7 clause (net count set by the first commitments; afterwards patterns start only in pairs of opposite sign) and the walk read as a field with filled negative states (identification with lattice QED). Entered: appendix C6 and C7; plain doc C7; MATTER; OPEN_DIRECTIONS (number change, now candidate, with its costs; the weak interaction added as a silence).

## 2026-11-11 — fields first or patterns first: number change read both ways (reading)

**The tension.** CO's instinct is that patterns make fields: a relation carries what is set by what passes (the branched-field ruling; the bound part of the which-way field is fixed by positions and currents). Number change (C6) reads the walk as a field with filled negative states, which looks like the 1930 direction, field first. Two readings of C6 are possible.

**Patterns first: Dirac's sea of actual patterns.** Every negative-energy state is held by an actual pattern; an antipattern is a hole. This is the particle-first form of the same physics (hole theory). On CO's lattice the sea is finite (2N negative states for N places with the four-component walk, two per place on average), so it breaks no infinity rule. A sea is possible only for spin ½ (it needs exclusion), and CO's elementary matter patterns are spin ½. It fails against rulings already made. New places are born on the possibility side, with no commitments (void births). Either a newborn place's negative states arrive empty, about two holes per place on average, so expansion makes antimatter everywhere at the birth rate (about 3H per place): its energy density alone, let alone its annihilation glow, rules that out. Or they arrive filled, which starts sea patterns other than in opposite-sign pairs, against C7's pairs clause (and makes births commitments, against the void-birth ruling). Borrowing from neighbours only moves the holes. The remaining escape, sea states that are inert and never renew, would not be patterns at all: that is the field reading. So the patterns-first reading of C6 is excluded, given void births and C7.

**Fields first.** Read C6 as a field: the walk's vacuum is a state of the field, a newborn place adds modes in that vacuum, and patterns and antipatterns are its excitations. This is compatible with CO's definition of a pattern as possibility in transit, a wave with commitments as the events on it (STATE_OF_CO, "Pattern"), though that definition is one-pattern-at-a-time: a field whose excitations are patterns, with number free, is the posit C6 adds.

**Where the instinct still holds.** The which-way field's Coulomb part, fixed at the same moment by the positions of the charges, is set by patterns, as in Coulomb-gauge electrodynamics (where the instantaneous Coulomb term is a constraint, not a degree of freedom). The Breit term comes from exchanged transverse light, and light has its own quanta on the capped links. So CO is field-first for possibility in transit, matter and light, and pattern-set only for the Coulomb part, which is the standard division in Coulomb gauge. The instinct "patterns make fields" survives as that constraint, not as a separate ontology.

**Grade.** Reading. The patterns-first reading of number change: excluded, given void births and C7's pairs clause. Fields first for possibility in transit: the reading of C6 to keep. The vacuum-energy cost of the filled states stands as before.

## 2026-11-11 — the semiclassical sector, quantified: the size, and a fork forced by no-signalling (reading)

**What CO has.** The free stock and h are classical, so a superposed mass sources gravity by its mean (Møller–Rosenfeld; Schrödinger–Newton in the Newtonian limit). Engagement resolves a pattern and conditions what shares its amplitude (the Lüders update, imported). A pattern left alone is not resolved (no collapse). Listed costs: Page–Geilker (a mean source for a body never resolved is excluded; CO escapes only if engagements resolve large bodies, the engagement criterion, a silence), and "the mean-field term must not signal".

**1. The size.** For a crystal, the centre-of-mass Schrödinger–Newton equation has the mean motion at the trap frequency and the quantum uncertainty rotating faster, at √(ω_c² + ω_SN²), with ω_SN ≈ 0.036 s⁻¹ for silicon near 10 K, enhanced over √(Gρ̄) by the concentration of mass at lattice sites, and larger for tungsten or osmium (Yang, Miao, Lee, Helou, Chen, PRL 110, 170401 (2013); a test proposed by Helou et al., PRD 96, 044008 (2017)). This is the number CO inherits while the mean source stands: untested, near reach.

**2. Signalling: the stance as written fails.** The Schrödinger–Newton equation together with the standard collapse (here, the Lüders update on engagement) allows faster-than-light signalling: Alice's measurement on one half of an entangled pair changes the mean source felt by Bob's half (Bahrami, Großardt, Donadi, Bassi, New J. Phys. 16, 115007 (2014)). CO has both ingredients: the mean source, and the Lüders update on engagement. So the cost "the mean-field term must not signal" is not open: as written, it signals. Bahrami et al.'s conclusion applies: a consistent semiclassical gravity needs a suitable prescription of collapse.

**3. The fork.** CO cannot keep all four of: (i) no collapse (a pattern left alone is not resolved); (ii) a mean gravitational source; (iii) the Lüders update on engagement; (iv) no signalling. One must go:
- *Drop (ii), quantise the source.* Gravity sourced by the quantum state, not its mean: no Schrödinger–Newton, no signalling, but gravity then entangles masses, and CO's stated falsifier flips (entanglement through gravity would become CO's prediction). It also needs the free stock and h to carry amplitude, against the ruling that free possibility has none.
- *Drop (i), let engagements be the source.* Gravity sourced by the engagement events themselves, as in the Tilloy–Diósi model where the stochastic localisation events source the potential: consistent, no signalling, no entanglement through gravity, and Page–Geilker answered (large bodies are resolved by the source itself). Cost: it is a collapse model, with a rate and a heating that experiments constrain (Diósi–Penrose-type tests, including the Gran Sasso bounds), and it replaces CO's "no collapse" prediction with a collapse one.
- *Drop (iii).* No conditioning on engagement: measurement as CO imports it goes.

**Reading.** The second branch is the one closest to CO's own picture (dents left by renewals and engagements in the free stock, read as the source), and it would turn the engagement criterion from a silence into the source of gravity's classical field. But it is a change of stance with testable cost. The first reverses the falsifier and conflicts with the no-amplitude ruling. The third discards imported measurement. This needs a ruling.

**Grade.** Reading. The present combination (no collapse, mean source, Lüders, no signalling) is excluded by the signalling argument; Schrödinger–Newton's size, ω_SN ≈ 0.036 s⁻¹ for silicon, stands only under the first-listed stance and is moot under the others.

**Ruled (2026-11-11, candidate): engagements source gravity.** Of the fork above, the second branch: gravity's classical source is set by engagements, not by the mean of an unresolved state.

## 2026-11-11 — engagements as gravity's source, worked out: two forms, and the one that keeps no collapse (reading)

**Two forms of the ruling.**
- *B1, gravity itself engages* (Tilloy–Diósi): the mass density is continuously, weakly measured by gravity, and the outcomes source the potential. Consistent and no-signalling, but it is a collapse model with a rate and a heating set by a smearing length R₀. The Gran Sasso test of gravity-related collapse (Donadi et al., Nature Physics 17, 74 (2021)) excludes the parameter-free form for R₀ below about 0.5 × 10⁻¹⁰ m. CO's own length is the hop, ℓ ≲ 1.3 × 10⁻³¹ m, twenty-one orders smaller: B1 with CO's scale is excluded, and with R₀ ≳ 0.5 Å it needs a length CO does not have.
- *B2, only the engagements already happening source gravity:* gravity adds no engagements of its own. Its source is the body's state given the record of its engagements (with light, with other patterns), evolved forward between them, as in the consistent Tilloy–Diósi form but without gravity's own engagements. Consistent: the source depends only on local outcomes, whose statistics do not depend on distant choices, so there is no signalling; the coupling is measurement followed by classical feedback, the standard consistent form of classical–quantum dynamics, so it carries the decoherence–diffusion trade-off (Oppenheim et al. 2023) with the decoherence supplied by the engagements themselves. It adds no collapse; the field noise the trade-off requires, and the small heating it brings, is set by how often a body is engaged, small for constantly engaged bodies. It is not Kafri–Taylor–Milburn, in which gravity makes its own measurements (B1-like, excluded in pairwise form: Altamirano et al. 2018); B2 uses only engagements that happen anyway.

**Reading: B2 is the form to keep.** It keeps three of the four (no collapse, the Lüders update, no signalling) and drops only the mean source, replacing it with the record of engagements. B1 is excluded at CO's scale.

**What B2 predicts.**
1. *No entanglement through gravity* (unchanged; Bose et al. 2017 and Marletto–Vedral 2017 would test it, and entanglement would make CO wrong).
2. *No self-gravity of an unresolved state:* an unengaged body's gravity does not follow its own wavefunction. A continuously monitored body is another matter: its source follows its conditioned state and acts back on it, a pull toward the recorded position of Schrödinger–Newton size, with noise; so the optomechanics test of Helou et al., whose mass is watched by a laser, is open under B2.
3. *Page–Geilker passed:* macroscopic bodies are engaged constantly (about 10¹⁹ thermal photons per second for a gram at room temperature, and about 10²³ gas collisions per second in air), so their source follows where they are.
4. *A new, presently untestable signature:* a body left unengaged gravitates from its record evolved forward, not from its present superposition.

**Costs, open.** The source jumps at each engagement, so energy and momentum are conserved only on average; the decoherence–diffusion trade-off sets a floor on how faithfully a weakly engaged body can source gravity, to be computed; "the record of engagements" needs the engagement criterion (which contacts are engagements), still a silence, now load-bearing for gravity too.

**Grade.** The ruling: candidate. B2: reading. The previous stance (mean source): excluded by signalling.

## 2026-11-11 — the engagement criterion, now load-bearing for gravity: a candidate statement (proposed for the ruling)

**Why now.** With gravity sourced by engagements (B2), the question "which contacts are engagements?" decides both when a pattern is resolved and when its gravity learns where it is. MATTER already holds two parts of an answer: a rate needs a final state broad against the coupling (|V| ≪ ħΓ, else passage stays coherent); and the chain a contact opens has a breadth in quanta, with a stopping number N (N ≳ 50).

**Candidate (for the ruling).** *A contact through a channel is an engagement when its final state is broad against the coupling and the chain it opens reaches N quanta; until then it is a passage, and can still be undone.* Both parts are already in CO; the candidate joins them into one criterion and makes it the single trigger for the Lüders update and for gravity's source.

**What it gives, read against cases.**
- *A thermal photon leaving a warm body:* emission into the continuum is broad (a rate), but one escaping photon is a chain of breadth one; it becomes an engagement where it is absorbed into a wall or detector whose cascade reaches N. So a body's position is resolved, and its gravity updated, through the environment's cascades, at rates far above what Page–Geilker needs.
- *A molecule in an interferometer* (10⁴–10⁵ atomic mass units, coherent): its contacts with stray gas or light are rare and, while it stays coherent, none reaches N; it is unresolved, and its gravity is not updated, consistent with the interference seen.
- *NMR echoes:* a correlated cluster below N quanta can be reversed (observed to about 10² spins under the qualifying Hamiltonian), so N ≳ 50 holds; the N test (MATTER_notes) is now also a test of when gravity updates.
- *A laser-monitored oscillator:* scattered light reaches photodetectors, whose cascades exceed N at once; so the mirror is engaged continuously, and B2's pull toward the recorded position applies (the optomechanics question, open).

**What it costs.** N is not derived; the criterion turns one unknown number into the trigger for measurement and gravity alike, which is economical (one number, two jobs) but makes N carry more. "Reaches N quanta" needs the breadth counted in a way that is the same for every observer (a frame-independent count): counting quanta in the chain's final state is invariant; counting places is not.

**Grade.** Proposed, shape: a joining of two existing clauses, with N its one unknown. If ruled, the silence "which contacts are engagements" becomes a candidate with one free number.

**Ruled (2026-11-11, candidate).** The engagement criterion as proposed above. Entered: appendix C8; MATTER ("Which contacts are engagements"; open: the value of N); SPACE_AND_GRAVITY ("Classical source").

*Cold-read notes on the ruling.* The count is in quanta (ruling 6, quanta not patterns); it is invariant for inertial observers and free final states, and how breadth is taken in a superposition of chains is still owed. The criterion relies on the earlier ruling that exchanges inside one body are passages; without it an isolated body whose internal chains pass N would engage itself.

## 2026-11-11 — B2's pull on a monitored oscillator: what the record would show (reading)

**Setup.** A mechanical oscillator (mass M, frequency ω_c) is measured continuously (record dy = ⟨x⟩_c dt + dW/(2√k)), with a thermal bath. Under B2 its gravity is sourced by its state given the record so far, the causally conditioned (filtered) state ρ_c. For a crystal, the self-gravity of a narrow state is a harmonic pull toward the source's centre (Yang et al. 2013):

H_SN = ½ M ω_SN² (x − ⟨x⟩_c)²,  ω_SN ≈ 0.036 s⁻¹ (Si, 10 K), 0.36–0.49 s⁻¹ for W, Os (Helou et al. 2017).

**The conditioned state stays Gaussian** (all terms linear or quadratic). Its mean obeys

d⟨x⟩_c = (⟨p⟩_c/M) dt + 2√k V_x dW,  d⟨p⟩_c = −(M ω_c² ⟨x⟩_c + γ⟨p⟩_c) dt − Mω_SN²(⟨x⟩_c − ⟨x⟩_c) dt + 2√k C_xp dW,

so the self-pull cancels in the mean: the pull is toward the mean itself. (⟨x⟩_c in H_SN is random but multiplies only a dt term, so there is no Itô correction.) It enters only the covariance (V_x, C_xp, V_p): the variance equations use ω_q² = ω_c² + ω_SN² where the mean's use ω_c², so the pull changes only the steady gains 2√k V_x, 2√k C_xp.

**So the record has no line at ω_q.** B2 assumes the record has Born statistics from the B2-evolved ρ_c, so dW is a Wiener process under B2's own law, and any other observer's filter sees the same record statistics. The record's spectrum is then set by the mean's dynamics (poles at ω_c, width γ) driven by the innovation with those gains: S_y(ω) = (1/4k)|1 + 2√k (iω − A)⁻¹ K|² in the usual notation, with A the mean's matrix (ω_c, γ) and K the gains. B2 changes K, not A: the height and shape of the feature at ω_c relative to shot noise shift, by a fraction of order ω_SN²/ω_c² when ω_c ≫ ω_SN and of order one for the low-frequency pendula these tests use (ω_c ~ ω_SN), and there is no new feature at ω_q. In steady state C_xp = 4kM V_x², so the gains depend on V_x alone, and a change of bath temperature reproduces the spectrum exactly at fixed k, γ and ω_c: the change is degenerate with temperature calibration.

**Against the published prescriptions.** Helou et al. (PRD 96, 044008 (2017)) find a feature at ω_q under both of their prescriptions: a peak when the potential is sourced by the incoming state (pre-selection), a dip when it is sourced by the state fixed at the end (post-selection). B2's causal conditioning is neither. It is the causal-conditional prescription worked out by Liu, Miao, Chen and Ma (arXiv:2207.05966), in which the mirror's state is conditionally prepared by the outgoing light and the potential feeds back along the measurement trajectory; this entry's result agrees with theirs that thermal noise hides the difference.

**Reading.** Under B2 the optomechanical tests of Schrödinger–Newton gravity would see no line at ω_q; a line there would count against B2 (and for a different source), and its absence would not distinguish B2 from standard quantum mechanics with quantum gravity unless the gains at ω_c are calibrated to a fraction ω_SN²/ω_c². B2 is therefore close to untestable with this method, as a self-consistent pull toward the conditional mean should be.

**Grade.** Reading (linear–Gaussian filtering; the thermal bath and back-action included in A and K). Not registered: the result follows analytically and the causal-conditional case is published (Liu et al., arXiv:2207.05966).

## 2026-11-12: locality relaxed to "local up to a fast-fading tail" (ruled, candidate)

**Before.** The walk and everything that couples to it were strictly local: each step reaches only neighbouring places (C6, run L1b). Three routes in the particle sketch stopped there: exact lattice chiral symmetry (Ginsparg–Wilson), a charge that differs between the staggered walk's two kinds, and a one-handed coupling to those kinds. Each needs a reach that falls off exponentially over a few spacings and never reaches exactly zero (OPEN_DIRECTIONS_notes/how_patterns_combine.md).

**Ruling.** Locality is "local up to a tail that fades exponentially at the hop scale": a coupling between places a distance d apart may be nonzero, bounded by C e^{−d/ξ} with ξ a few hops. The free walk may stay strictly local. Signals still travel at c, up to leakage of order e^{−d/ξ}, which is nothing at any measurable distance. This is the standard of locality in lattice field theory (Hernández, Jansen and Lüscher 1999). Grade: the ruling, candidate.

**What it opens.** Route 1 is reopened. The staggered walk's free kind label (reach falling ≈ 0.3 per cell over the first cells, then a slow tail; exponential reach not shown) can carry a kind-dependent charge, and its left and right kind rotations can carry a one-handed coupling (the weak interaction's form: permitted at the free level, not predicted; the kind-blind chirality cannot be exact and local, by Nielsen–Ninomiya and the anomaly). These openings do not meet the ruling's own e^{−d/ξ} standard (cold read): the exact kind label is twisted over the momentum zone (the kinds swap going around along two axes), so its reach falls as a power, and a strictly local label fails at the hop scale. A kind-dependent charge is then nearly exact but not exactly conserved at ℓ, and keeping light massless would need tuning (how_patterns_combine.md, "The tail, found").

**Owed.** A cold read; the speed check (below); the results docs (TECHNICAL_APPENDIX C6, MATTER "The step") after the cold read.

**Speed check, staggered walk ([`staggered_speed_check.py`](NUCLEUS_notes/staggered_speed_check.py)).** The fading tail touches only couplings, not free motion, so the question is the staggered walk's own speeds. The mass enters its dispersion only as m²:

E(q, m)² − E(q, 0)² = θ_m² (1 − O(q²)),

which holds to eight digits at θ_m = 10⁻³ and 10⁻², with ratios 0.99997, 0.9992, 0.9969, 0.9877, 0.951 at q = 0.01, 0.05, 0.1, 0.2, 0.4 (θ = 0.5). There is no term linear in the mass: a massive pattern's speed at q ≫ θ_m follows the Lorentz form, v = v₀(1 − θ_m²/2E₀²), to within 3 × 10⁻⁴ θ_m (finite-difference noise). The earlier "top speed below light's by (0.46–0.81)θ_m" was the peak of the curve where mass and lattice curvature meet, not a shift of the limiting speed. That earlier figure is withdrawn. The Wilson walk's limiting-speed excess, s ≈ 0.24 r mcℓ/ħ, came from its momentum-dependent mass. It is what made the Crab bound ℓ ≲ 1.3 × 10⁻³¹ m. The staggered walk has no such term, so for a staggered pattern the leading departures are the lattice's (kℓ)² terms, bounded through quadratic (n = 2) tests. Correction (cold read): the massless dispersion bends below light's at (kℓ)² (−0.032 q² on an axis, −0.062 q² on the diagonal, θ = 0.5), and that term equals the Wilson walk's mass-linear one at k ≈ √(m/ℓ), about 1 PeV for the electron, the Crab and LHAASO range. Unless light bends the same way, subluminal electrons are bounded there (photon decay, synchrotron cutoff) at a comparable scale, roughly ℓ ≲ 3 × 10⁻³¹ m. So staggering electrons changes the bound's form, not its size; the bound depends on light's own dispersion. Not yet computed.

**Then (same day): C6 held exact (ruled).** The kind-dependent charge is excluded within CO rather than priced with a tuning of light's mass; the staggered walk is held as a shape for the quark sector, its two kinds sharing one charge. The locality ruling stands as a candidate; what it was meant to open (charge by kind, the one-handed coupling) does not open on the staggered walk, because the obstacle is the label's twist, not locality.

## 2026-11-12 — routes to N, revisited: where an upper bound could come from (reading; shape)

**Why.** N sets measurement (C8), gravity's source (B2) and CO's one distinct prediction. Earlier entries closed two routes to deriving it: ties to CO's constants (N = 1/δ and kin need a new, erasable record that clashes with four standing rules) and the web's spare capacity (fails the detector test). It has a lower bound only (N ≳ 50, conditional). This entry asks where an upper bound could come from.

**1. Not from experiments that match quantum mechanics.** Without reversal, CO and quantum mechanics predict the same thing: a chain that is not reversed decoheres in CO exactly as it does in quantum mechanics, whatever N is. So every confirmed measurement, every decoherence rate and every record bounds nothing. A detector's chain does commit eventually, but records grow without limit (amplifier, wire, memory, reader), so "the record exists" gives only N ≲ the largest chain anywhere. The same goes for Page–Geilker-type gravity tests under B2: the mass is moved by a macroscopic chain. And because breadth grows by multiplication (time ∝ ln N), timing gives nothing either. The only experimental upper bound possible is the N test itself: a reversal ceiling fixed in breadth and the same across platforms.

**2. Not from a cap on the joint amplitude ("no infinities" applied to entanglement).** The route: a chain is one pattern with one undivided stock (entanglement candidate). Its joint amplitude over N two-way branchings has up to 2^N components. If a finite stock S can hold only S/s distinguishable components, then

2^N ≤ S/s,  so  N ≤ log₂(S/s),

which would make N naturally modest (a logarithm) and derived. Tried with CO's own ratio, S/s = P_max/(δħ) = (ℓ/ℓ_P)²/4π ≲ 5 × 10⁶ (hop bound): N ≤ 22, against N ≳ 50. With S/s the number of hop-scale places in a pattern's Compton volume, (λ_C/ℓ)³: N ~ 150–230 (electron), 150–190 (proton), over the allowed range of ℓ. But any such cap limits entanglement in general, not only engagement chains, and the double-quantum NMR echoes reverse multiple-quantum coherences over clusters of ~10⁴ spins, which are joint amplitudes far beyond 2^230 components. (Those echoes are excluded from the N test because the drive supplies the quanta, not because the clusters lack many components.) Superconducting processors keep random-circuit states of 50–70 qubits in line with quantum mechanics. **Excluded** as a route to N, unless the cap is restricted to chains through broad final states, which would be a rule made for the purpose. Gained: N does not live in how much amplitude a pattern can hold. "No infinities" sets no logarithmic ceiling on entanglement anywhere near the lab's reach.

**3. What is left.** What sets an engagement apart is its broad final state (|V| ≪ ħΓ); N is how far the chain through it must spread. The remaining routes would tie N to the final state's continuum (how many modes within the width a chain must reach), not to the pattern or to CO's constants. None is worked out. Until one is, N is a fourth constant of CO's own, bounded below by experiment (≳ 50, conditional on ruling 3 and the warm-sample posits) and above by nothing but a universal reversal ceiling, if one exists.

**Grade.** Reading. The amplitude-cap route is excluded; experiments matching quantum mechanics cannot bound N from above (a consequence of CO's equivalence to quantum mechanics without reversal). N stays a shape.

## 2026-11-12 — the continuum route to N (reading; closes as a derivation, leaves a dilemma)

**The route.** An engagement's distinguishing mark is its broad final state. Could N be a count set by that continuum, such as how many modes within the width a chain must reach?

**The broad clause is already a count.** With the golden rule, Γ = 2π|V|²ρ/ħ (ρ the density of final states), the number of final states within the width is

n_Γ = ħΓρ = 2π|V|²ρ²,  and  ħΓ/|V| = 2π|V|ρ,  so  n_Γ = (ħΓ/|V|)² / 2π.

"Broad against the coupling" (|V| ≪ ħΓ) is the same as "one quantum shared over many final states" (n_Γ ≫ 1). C8 therefore holds two thresholds, not one: how many modes a single quantum's final state must span (the "≪", unset) and how many quanta the chain must reach (N). Both say "spread over many"; they count different things (modes, quanta).

**Why the continuum cannot set N.** Every count the continuum offers, n_Γ, the ratio of the recurrence (Heisenberg) time ħρ to the decay time 1/Γ (which is the same number, ħρΓ = n_Γ), or the modes reached by a chain of given breadth, depends on the system's coupling and density of states. These vary by many orders of magnitude between a nucleus, an atom in free space and a spin in a crystal. A number built from them is system-dependent. The N test, though, rests on N being the same everywhere: that is what separates CO from emergent irreversibility, whose ceilings are set by each system's dynamics. Two cases follow.
- N universal: the continuum cannot supply it; CO's constants cannot either (2026-11-09: the ties need a new, erasable record); so N is a fourth constant, set by experiment.
- N system-dependent, set by the continuum: then CO's ceiling shifts from system to system as emergent irreversibility's does, and the universality row of the test no longer separates them. N stops being a distinct prediction.

**Folding the two thresholds into one (a poke, excluded).** Requiring the chain's breadth in modes, not quanta, to reach N would make one threshold do both jobs. But a single photon emitted into free space spans an unbounded number of modes, so every spontaneous emission would engage at once, and ruling 6 counts quanta precisely to avoid the resonator exclusion (one phonon moving 10¹³–10¹⁷ atoms). Excluded.

**Grade.** Reading. The continuum route closes as a derivation of a universal N. What is gained is the dilemma, stated sharply: N is either a fourth constant of CO's own, set by experiment, or not a prediction at all. Also stated: C8's "≪" is a second, unset threshold (in modes).

## 2026-11-12 — N posited as a constant of CO's own (ruled)

Given the dilemma above (a universal N cannot come from the continuum or from CO's other constants; a system-dependent N is not a prediction), ruled: **N is a constant of CO's own, posited, set only by experiment.** It is the fourth, beside δ, P_max and Λ. Present bound N ≳ ~50, conditional on ruling 3 and the warm-sample posits; no upper bound except a universal reversal ceiling, if one exists. Routes closed on the way: ties to the other constants (needs an erasable record), the web's spare capacity (fails the detector test), a cap on the joint amplitude (excluded by reversed NMR clusters), the continuum (system-dependent). What it keeps: CO's one distinct prediction, the same N in every system. Results docs updated (README, STATE_OF_CO, MATTER, SPACE_AND_GRAVITY, TECHNICAL_APPENDIX, OPEN_DIRECTIONS, SCOREBOARD).

## 2026-11-12 — C8's broad clause made sharp: |V| < ħΓ/4 (ruled, candidate)

**Before.** C8 called a final state broad when |V| ≪ ħΓ: a second threshold, never set (continuum entry above).

**The line standard physics supplies.** A long-lived source amplitude a coupled with strength g = |V|/ħ to a final state b that loses its amplitude at rate Γ/2 (energy width ħΓ):

da/dt = −i g b,  db/dt = −i g a − (Γ/2) b,  so  λ = −Γ/4 ± √((Γ/4)² − g²).

For g > Γ/4 the roots are complex: the quantum oscillates back and forth (vacuum Rabi splitting, coherent passage). For g < Γ/4 they are real: it only leaks away, and for g ≪ Γ/4 at the golden-rule rate 4g²/Γ = 4|V|²/(ħ²Γ), the rate MATTER already quotes. With a source of its own width γ the line is g = |Γ − γ|/4 and the weak rate becomes 4g²/(Γ + γ). This is the exceptional point (for γ = 0 also cavity physics' usual strong–weak boundary; with γ, strong coupling is often quoted as g > (Γ + γ)/4, a resolvable splitting), not a new constant.

**Ruling.** The broad clause reads |V| < ħΓ/4: the contact does not oscillate back. Engagement = no oscillating back, and a chain reaching N quanta. C8 now has one free number, N, posited. Docs updated: MATTER (Channels; Which contacts are engagements), TECHNICAL_APPENDIX C8, OPEN_DIRECTIONS.

## 2026-11-12 — chains inside one body count toward N (ruled, restating 9 Nov)

**The contradiction found.** The 9 Nov ruling made exchanges between patterns inside one body passages that count toward a chain's breadth; the N test (one crystal's spins) and the bound N ≳ ~50 rest on it. MATTER and OPEN_DIRECTIONS said instead that "an isolated body does not engage itself and stays unresolved however its internal chains grow", and the cold-read note on C8 (11 Nov) leaned the same way. Read that way, the N test in one crystal could never show N, and the bound would bound nothing.

**Ruled.** The 9 Nov reading stands: exchanges between patterns inside one body are passages and count toward the chain. A single pattern left alone is never resolved; a body of many patterns commits internally once a chain inside it reaches N. The place of an internal commitment is an independent draw that records nothing about where the body is (ruling 7), so molecule interferometry is unaffected, and gravity's source (B2) stays where the last outside engagement put it (an internal record carries no place). Cold read: condition 1 (|V| < ħΓ/4) for spins in a crystal pairs one dipolar coupling J against a local-field width ~√z·J, so it can hold in dense 3D solids and fails in few-spin clusters (ferrocene's mesoscopic echoes); the N test assumes it, checked by local polarization decaying without oscillating back (added to the N note). MATTER and OPEN_DIRECTIONS corrected; the 11 Nov cold-read note is superseded.

## 2026-11-12 — the frame problem: tuning accepted for now (ruled)

**Before.** The author had not accepted tuning (11 Nov). The negative-space map (OPEN_DIRECTIONS_notes/negative_space.md) found interactions on the lattice under 12 of 21 open items. The day's readings (frame note) then closed the remaining exits for CO's network as it stands:
- a common origin for walk and links: induced fields split at about 1/ln(1/mℓ);
- a deformed Lorentz symmetry: exact only for free patterns near long wavelength; additive lattice momentum allows no continuous boosts;
- boosts on pairs with backreaction: equivalent to equal one-loop speed shifts, which Wilson-type lattices do not have.
What was left was a fork: tune, or a network whose coordinates do not commute.

**Ruled.** Tuning is accepted for now. The loop counter-terms that keep species' top speeds equal, to the 10⁻²⁰ the data require (|ξ_e − ξ_γ| ≲ 10⁻¹⁷ at the dimension-four level), are set by hand, named in "What goes in" beside the four constants. This is not a fifth constant in the same sense: it is a set of counter-terms, one per pair of sectors, fixed by requiring equal speeds. The door to non-commuting coordinates stays open as a direction. The frame problem is no longer counted as the open problem blocking the items it sat under; they are now conditional on the tuning.

**Lakatos.** Tuning is accommodating by definition: added to fit, buys nothing new. Counts at the end of 12 November: progressive 11, accommodating 3, neutral 15.

Results docs updated: README ("What goes in"), STATE_OF_CO, OPEN_DIRECTIONS (Lorentz invariance with loops), TECHNICAL_APPENDIX, SCOREBOARD.

## 2026-11-13 — B1: does a bound pattern's speed excess follow its parts or its whole mass?

Registered (MATTER_runs/B1_bound_excess/REGISTRATION.md): two patterns on the 1D walk held by a linear potential. Pre-check passed (a free pair gives s(θ) exactly). The confined cases did not confine: a linear vector potential (a phase) lets Dirac patterns escape through negative energy (Klein), so the criteria were not met (neither). A scalar potential would confine but would put the binding into mass-flip energy, giving "whole" by construction; in CO's nucleus the binding is the colour field on links. By import (Gagnon and Moore 2004): a composite's limiting speed is a momentum-weighted average over its parts, intensive. So the excess per nucleon is at most of order the nucleon's, from the share tied to quark masses, and does not scale with a nucleus's whole mass; MICROSCOPE gives no bound on ℓ beyond the Crab. **Grade: reading, by an imported result; the walk's own link-bound state not computed.**

## 2026-11-14 — The Wilson flip's window: L1b's gap check corrected

**The error.** L1b and G6 checked the extra species' gap on grids (40³, 24³). A continuous search (Nelder–Mead from grid starts, cold read 14 Nov, confirmed) finds the gap closed at w = 0.3: twelve points on the face diagonals, k ≈ (0, ±0.531π, ±0.531π) and permutations, at quasi-energy 0 (gap 7 × 10⁻¹⁴), and the π sector closed too. The grids stepped over the crossings. Along an axis there is a proof: U = A_x², and A_x's eigenphase runs from 0 at k = 0 to π − 2w at k = π, so for w < π/4 it passes π/2 and U has eigenvalue −1. So at w = 0.3 the walk carries extra massless species.

**The window.** At a corner with n components at π the step is one flip, F(θ − 4nw), so those species sit at quasi-energy 4nw mod 2π (θ → 0) and turn massless where 4nw is a multiple of π. Above π/4, the continuous search finds no crossing off the corners (w = 0.85, 0.9, 5π/16, 1.0, 1.04, 1.067, 1.10, 1.12–1.17); the n = 3 corner closes at the single point w = π/3 (gap 0.086 at w = 1.04, open again at 1.067), and the n = 2 corner closes at 3π/8, beyond which a closed line spreads from it off the corner (found at (π, 0.231π, π) at w = 1.2). **Needed: π/4 < w < 3π/8, w ≠ π/3.** The smallest corner gap is largest, π/4, at w = 5π/16 (corners at quasi-energies 5π/4, π/2, −π/4; the smallest extra-species gap anywhere 0.70 per step); a value within the window is a choice.

**What changes.**
- The speed excess is s = κ_w mcℓ/ħ with κ_w = ½ sin w (cold read, numerical to 10⁻⁴), 0.35 to 0.43 in the window. κ_w is 0.35 to 0.46 across the window. The Crab bound becomes ℓ ≲ 5 × 10⁻³² m (4.2 to 5.5 × 10⁻³² across it; 4.6 × 10⁻³² at 5π/16) and δ ≳ 10⁻⁶ (1.5 × 10⁻⁶ at 5π/16). The walk's quadratic dispersion along an axis (−0.086 (kℓ)² at 5π/16) lowers a PeV electron's excess by about 10% at that ℓ.
- Light's speed on the walk is 2cos w: 0.77 to 1.41 hops per step (1.11 at 5π/16). The headroom against the strict reach of 2 grows to 40–100%; the Wilson flip can speed light only until w reaches π/4, by up to 27% at 5π/16. **Open: slowing.** Under P7f a field that slows light raises w: from 5π/16, a slowing of about 10% reaches π/3, where the n = 3 corner turns massless, and about 31% reaches 3π/8. Near compact stars (lapse about 0.77 at a neutron star's surface) that is crossed, so in a shell where w = π/3 one extra species would be massless. Not yet assessed.
- The extra species (G9, rerun at 5π/16, θ = 0.05): for a 1% slower clock their rest energies change by −1.13%, +3.5%, −9.6% (n = 1, 2, 3); equivalence needs −1%. Still an order-one violation for n = 2 and 3, so G9's reading and "not particles" stand.
- Runs at w = 0.3 whose forms do not depend on the extra species stand (G5, G6, G7, G8, G12, G13: slow or long-wavelength patterns, built from the light band); their numbers (c₀, κ_w) move with w. G6's "the flip can speed light up by at most about 1%" is withdrawn (it rested on the grid check).

Cold read applied (14 Nov): the window widened to π/4 < w < 3π/8 except π/3; the off-corner wording; the n = 2 closing quoted at 3π/8; the slowing limit under P7f noted as open.

## 2026-11-14 — Light's lattice form (ruled) and what it decides at high energy (reading)

**The question.** Light's speed at long wavelength is the walk's c₀ (posited). Its next term, how its speed falls at short wavelength, depends on the lattice form of the link field's own cost, which CO had not fixed. At ultra-high energy that term, set against the walk's own, decides whether charged matter outruns light.

**The numbers** (w = 5π/16; group speed v = c₀(1 + c₂(kℓ)²)):

| direction | matter (the massless walk) | light as a separate link field (standard lattice form) | light as two walk modes sharing its momentum, ω(k) = 2E_walk(k/2) |
|---|---|---|---|
| axis | −0.086 | −0.125 (light slower by 0.039) | −0.022 (light faster by 0.065) |
| face diagonal | −0.068 | −0.063 (faster by 0.005) | −0.017 (faster by 0.051) |
| body diagonal | −0.062 | −0.042 (faster by 0.020) | −0.015 (faster by 0.046) |

**Light as a separate link field: excluded by cosmic rays** (for every allowed ℓ). Along the lattice's axes, matter would outrun light by 0.039 (kℓ)², and a charged particle faster than light radiates above E_th, where the speed excess equals (mc²/E)²/2. At the Crab bound on ℓ (ħc/ℓ ≈ 4 × 10¹⁵ GeV), quarks in a 10²⁰ eV proton (kℓ ~ 5 × 10⁻⁶) exceed the threshold by about 10¹⁰, and those in an iron nucleus at 10²⁰ eV by about 10³. Electrons at 10¹⁷ eV in air showers exceed it too, even at the smallest ℓ allowed (3.5 ℓ_P): 3 × 10⁻²³ against 1.3 × 10⁻²³.

**Light as two walk modes: safe for matter, with a photon-decay threshold.** Light is faster than matter in every direction (by 0.046 to 0.065 (kℓ)²), so charged matter never outruns it. Light faster than electrons can split into an electron pair above E⁴ ≈ 4(mₑc²)²(ħc/ℓ)²/Δc₂, about 4 PeV at the Crab bound on ℓ, rising as ℓ falls (1.4 × 10⁸ GeV at 3.5 ℓ_P). Photons are seen to 1.4 PeV (LHAASO), so this is not yet excluded, and photons above a few PeV would bound ℓ through it. This is the quantum-walk theory of light (Bisio, D'Ariano and Perinotti 2016, light as a pair of Weyl walks).

**Ruled.** Light hops as matter hops: its dispersion is that of two walk modes sharing its momentum, ω_light(k) = 2E_walk(k/2). Ruled, candidate. Open: how this sits with light as the U(1) link phase of the which-way field (the link field still carries the phase; the ruling fixes its lattice dynamics to the walk's), and the photon-decay threshold as a test.

Grade: reading (the two options' consequences); the ruling, candidate (the choice).

## 2026-11-14 — The Wilson flip's value (ruled)

**Ruled.** w = 5π/16, the value in the window π/4 < w < 3π/8 (w ≠ π/3) that gives the extra species their largest smallest gap, π/4 at the corners (0.70 per step anywhere in the zone). Ruled, posited. With it: c₀ = 2cos(5π/16) = 1.111 hops per step; κ_w = ½ sin(5π/16) = 0.416; the Crab bound ℓ ≲ 4.6 × 10⁻³² m, δ ≳ 1.5 × 10⁻⁶.

## 2026-11-14 — Light's lattice form: the cold read's corrections (reading)

**Corrections to the entry above.**
- *Photon decay happens through lopsided splits,* not because light outruns electrons at equal momentum: at an even split the photon has exactly the energy of two massless walk modes, so mass and the speed excess s forbid it. With s kept, the threshold is E_th = C√(mₑc²·ħc/ℓ), C = 6.2, 6.8, 7.1 (axis, face, body) for two-mode light (exact walk numerics and analysis agree). At the Crab bound on ℓ that is 9–10.5 PeV, not 4–4.5; at 3.5 ℓ_P, 2.6–3.0 × 10¹⁷ eV.
- *The highest photon seen is about 2.5 PeV* (LHAASO, Cygnus bubble), not 1.4. Published photon-decay bounds assume a superluminal photon and standard electrons and do not apply as written; threshold against highest photon is the clean comparison. Not excluded.
- *"Charged matter never outruns light" is wrong for composites.* A proton or nucleus bends with its partons' x³-weighted share of the walk's c₂, about 0.005 c₂ (Gagnon and Moore 2004), while two-mode light bends with c₂/4. So protons and nuclei outrun two-mode light at high momentum, and the hard-emission threshold, E_th ≈ (27/(8|β_γ|))^{1/4}√(Mc²·ħc/ℓ) (β_γ = c₂/12), is 3 × 10¹⁷ to 9 × 10¹⁸ eV for protons and 2 × 10¹⁸ to 7 × 10¹⁹ eV for iron across the allowed ℓ, below the 1–2 × 10²⁰ eV cosmic rays seen. **Two-mode light is likely excluded** by the highest-energy cosmic rays, unless the emission rate just above threshold rescues it (not computed).
- *The link-field exclusion* holds for protons and nuclei with the kinematic criterion (open on the lattice's axes only, at 6.06√(mΛ) without s); the 10¹⁷ eV air-shower electron sentence is withdrawn (with s the off-axis electron band exists for either light form; it is the Crab bound itself).
- *A link field cannot have ω = 2E_walk(k/2) exactly* (period 4π in k); only its quadratic term can be matched, direction by direction (c₂(n̂) = A + BΣnᵢ⁴, by next-nearest link terms). And the Bisio–D'Ariano–Perinotti photon is a pair of Weyl automata on the BCC lattice; CO would borrow only its dispersion form.
- *Side finding:* on the axis, c₂ of the walk is exactly −κ_w²/2 = −sin²w/8 (to 10⁻⁸ at w = 0.85, 0.98, 1.1), so along the axis an electron just touches c₀; off axis the quadratic term raises the soft-Cherenkov threshold from 1.10 to about 1.27–1.29 √(mΛ), loosening the Crab bound slightly (to about 6 × 10⁻³² m on the body diagonal).

**What the cosmic rays ask for.** Composites bend with c₂ near zero, so light must bend no more than they do. **Light running straight** (c₂ = 0 in every direction, an improved link action) passes: light is then faster than electrons and composites at high momentum (no Cherenkov from the quadratic term), and photon decay opens at E_th = 5.4, 5.9, 6.1 √(mₑc²·ħc/ℓ) (axis, face, body): 8–9 PeV at the Crab bound on ℓ, 2.3–2.6 × 10¹⁷ eV at 3.5 ℓ_P. Photons are seen to 2.5 PeV. A photon above about 9 PeV would bound ℓ below the Crab bound; a confirmed photon above about 3 × 10¹⁷ eV would exclude it at every allowed ℓ. Grade: reading; the ruled form (two-mode light) is likely excluded, and the straight form is the candidate replacement, for the ruling.

**Ruled.** Light runs straight: the link field's own cost takes an improved lattice form under which light's group speed has no quadratic term in any direction, v_light = c₀ + O((kℓ)⁴). Ruled, candidate. It replaces "light hops as matter hops" (likely excluded by the highest-energy cosmic rays, above).

## 2026-11-14 — Straight light: the cold read (reading)

**The Crab bound was a factor 2 loose.** Li and Ma bound δ in E² = m² + p²(1 + δ), with δ = 2(v − 1): their δ ≲ 5 × 10⁻²⁰ means a speed excess s < 2.45 × 10⁻²⁰ at 2.31 PeV. With straight light and the walk's quadratic term kept, the body diagonal binds (band from 1.261√(mₑc²ħc/ℓ)): **ℓ ≲ 3.0 × 10⁻³² m, δ ≳ 3.5 × 10⁻⁶** (s alone would give 2.3 × 10⁻³² m). The figure 4.6–5 × 10⁻³² m in README, MATTER, TECHNICAL_APPENDIX, SCOREBOARD and the two entries above is to be corrected.

**Checked and passing (straight light).** Photon decay E_th = C√(mₑc²·ħc/ℓ), C = 5.37, 5.89, 6.10 (axis, face, body; collinear, lopsided, soft share x ≈ 0.08–0.09; θ-independent): at the corrected Crab bound 10–11 PeV, at 3.5 ℓ_P 2.3–2.6 × 10¹⁷ eV. Protons, nuclei and pions: no vacuum Cherenkov from the quadratic terms (composites bend with c₂ Σ⟨x³⟩ ≈ −4 × 10⁻⁴; their s is far below the barrier). Electrons: the off-axis s-band, 1.26–2.9√(mₑc²ħc/ℓ), is the Crab bound and the only electron channel. Muons: the same band from about 33 PeV at the Crab bound (a silence). Neutrino Cherenkov, pair emission and splitting are forbidden; photon time of flight and γ → 3γ give nothing measurable (silences).

**Conditions.**
- *The tuning rule.* The coefficients C = 5.4–6.1 hold if s survives the counter-terms. If the counter-terms absorb s into each species' speed, C = 3.8–4.1, the electron band and the Crab bound vanish, and LHAASO's 2.5 PeV photon gives ℓ < 2.3 × 10⁻³¹ m. "A confirmed photon above about 3 × 10¹⁷ eV excludes straight light at every allowed ℓ" holds under either rule.
- *B1's import.* If a composite's s followed its whole mass, protons would have a band even with straight light (10¹⁷–3 × 10¹⁸ eV at the Crab bound), excluded wherever protons are seen.
- *Gravity's quadratic term.* Gravity couples to the whole proton; if the gravity sector bent like an unimproved field, protons would outrun gravitons by about 0.1(E/Λ)² (gravitational Cherenkov; Moore and Nelson 2001; Kostelecký and Tasson 2015). Gravity needs the same inequality as light at most allowed ℓ: a silence with a cosmic-ray constraint pending. The gluon link enters composites only through ⟨x³⟩_g ≈ 6 × 10⁻⁴ and changes nothing.

**The ruling, restated.** Needed: light's quadratic term no lower than composites', c₂_L ≳ −4 × 10⁻⁴ in every direction (highest-energy cosmic rays, given the Gagnon–Moore import). Posited (ruled): c₂_L = 0 inside that window, polarisation-degenerate. It is a choice and a tuning: it sets two cubic coefficients (A = B = 0 in c₂(n̂) = A + BΣnᵢ⁴) against an unimproved value near 0.1, and as a renormalised condition (walk loops feed matter's bending into light at roughly 10⁻³–10⁻²) it is a second tuning beside the speed counter-terms. It is achievable: a coordinated electric-and-magnetic improvement (smearing with k̃ = (9/8)·2 sin(k/2) − (1/8)(2/3) sin(3k/2)) gives c₂ = 0 for both polarisations in every direction; a magnetic-only improvement would make light birefringent, which MeV polarimetry of gamma-ray bursts would test near the Crab bound. The link field's time scheme is part of the form and still to be named (a nearest-neighbour leapfrog cannot run at c₀ = 1.111 hops per step; stepping it in the palindrome's half-steps is stable and bends as (cos²w − Σnᵢ⁴)/8, still too much). Two-mode light: excluded, conditional on the emission rate just above threshold (not computed).

**The test, restated.** Photons split into an electron pair above E_th = C√(mₑc²·ħc/ℓ), C = 5.4–6.1 by direction if s survives the counter-terms (3.8–4.1 if not): 10–11 PeV at the Crab bound, 2.3–2.6 × 10¹⁷ eV at 3.5 ℓ_P. LHAASO's highest photon is 2.5 PeV. A photon above about 11 PeV would bound ℓ below the Crab bound; a confirmed photon above about 3 × 10¹⁷ eV excludes straight light at every allowed ℓ under either rule (Auger and TA ultra-high-energy photon searches; their non-detection so far is consistent, not confirmation). **Grade: candidate test, conditional on the tuning rule, B1's import and gravity's quadratic term.**

## 2026-11-14 — Tuning 2: the domain-wall walk (B2, B2b)

**Why.** Wilson-type walks break chiral symmetry, so each fermion mass takes an additive loop shift needing a counter-term (tuning 2). CO's palindromic walk has no hidden chiral symmetry (σx, σy, σz all fail ΓUΓ = U†). Two known routes: an overlap walk (exponential tails; continuous-time construction) or a domain-wall walk (an extra inner direction; strictly local).

**Result (MATTER_runs/B2_domain_wall).** On CO's own 2D walk at w = 5π/16, with the inner direction s carrying a θ domain wall (±0.5), each wall binds one one-way mode, the pair moving at c₀. CO's matter sign (small θ > 0) is the topological phase (Chern −1). Undisturbed, the leftover mass falls as e^(−0.38L). Under a disturbance that gives the plain walk a mass of 0.99ε (coherent plus random spin rotations, no symmetry kept, uniform in x), the mass keeps falling with L (B2b: m(24) ≈ 10⁻⁵ at ε = 0.2). B2's second condition failed as registered (bar mis-set below the undisturbed residual); B2b replaced it with the leak-versus-protection signature (plateau against fall), registered before running, and passed.

**Ruled.** Tuning 2 is removed by a domain-wall walk: CO's matter is the light mode bound to a wall in a short inner direction at each place, and a physical mass enters as a direct coupling between the two walls. Ruled, candidate. Still to show: disturbances varying in x and time, interactions, and the 3+1D form (a 4+1D walk).

The disturbed cases' weight transfer (sudden switch-on of the disturbance) is untested as an explanation.

The registered control (spin-keeping) was uninformative (1.5×10⁻³); a spin-turning control, fixed and declared before rerunning it alone, gives 5.5×10⁻². **Limit (cold read):** every case found one crossing. With period 8 the folded crossings sit at quasienergy ±c₀π/8 ≈ ±0.44, outside the window, so only the zero-momentum crossing was probed, which sees the x-average of the disturbance. Coupling through the disturbance's x-momentum is not tested; a period ≥ 32 would put folded crossings in the window.

**Cold read, corrections.**
- *Speed is not protected.* At L = 16, ε = 0.2, a coherent disturbance lowers both walls' speeds by 2.4%; a random draw gives the two walls (the two handednesses) speeds differing by 1.2%. Set locally at each wall, not suppressed by e^(−αL). Only the mass counter-term is removed; speed counter-terms remain (tuning 1), now per handedness.
- *Rate.* At large L, α ≈ 0.39 per place undisturbed and at ε = 0.2 (checked to L = 32, no plateau); fits over L = 8–24 give 0.46–0.49, pulled up by short L.
- *The 130.* The leftover mass falls below mₑ (at ℓ = ℓ_P) for L ≳ 130 (a ring of about 260 inner places) at this wall height. mₑ itself enters as the wall-to-wall coupling, which must turn spin.
- *Conditions.* The protection holds while loop-shifted θ and w stay in their phases (θ_in in (0, ~1.6), θ_out in (−1.5, 0), both the 0-gap and π-gap open); renormalising them changes α, a factor, not an additive tuning. The w window was not mapped. With gauge fields, lattice-QCD experience is that the leftover mass falls more slowly than e^(−αL) in rough backgrounds.
- *Topology.* Chern −1 is relative to C = 0 for θ < 0; the 0-gap count is the Floquet winding W₀, and one mode per wall follows because the π-gap stays open across the wall (smallest half-gap 0.285). W_π was not computed.

**The ruling, restated.** Tuning 2 (the mass counter-term) is removed by a domain-wall walk; a physical mass enters as a spin-turning wall-to-wall coupling. Ruled, candidate. Not removed: speed counter-terms, now per handedness. Still to show: x-variation through a longer period, the w window, interactions, the 3+1D form.

## 2026-11-14 — Domain-wall walk: the second cold read (reading)

**The symmetry was misidentified; the claim it carried is withdrawn.** The bare reflection s → L − 1 − s is not a symmetry of the walk (reversing the s-hops needs the components swapped, which reverses the x-hops). The operator that holds the two walls' speeds equal is antiunitary: Θ = (iσ_yK)·(s → L − 1 − s)·(x → −x), ΘUΘ⁻¹ = U; it sends quasienergy E on wall 1 to −E on wall 2 at the same k, so v₂ = −v₁. Every determinant-1 spin rotation satisfies σ_yP*σ_y = P, so B2f's random-direction disturbances were invariant automatically: the test covered x-uniform, static spin rotations only. Counterexamples: a potential-type disturbance e^(−iεφ_s) symmetric under s → L − 1 − s splits the speeds by 3.9–4.1×10⁻³ at L = 8, 16, 24 (ε = 0.2), not falling; an x-varying spin disturbance not mirror-symmetric in x breaks the pairing by 1.2×10⁻². With gauge fields Θ needs charge conjugation as well: a CP-type symmetry, which QED and QCD can keep and the weak interaction breaks; chiral gauge couplings would renormalise the handednesses separately in any case (and chiral gauge theories on domain walls are a known open problem). **Withdrawn:** "loop corrections that respect CO's rules cannot split the two handednesses' speeds; tuning 1 stays one speed counter-term per species." Corrected: the handednesses' speeds are not protected; speed counter-terms are needed per handedness. The ruling recorded in the entry above (the domain-wall rule keeps the inner reflection) rested on this misidentification; open to re-ruling.

**Other corrections.**
- *w window:* counting wall states directly (L = 16, > 80% weight within 4 places of a wall, inside 0.7× the bulk gap), wall modes appear at quasienergy π below about 4.45π/16 (with modes at both 0 and π at 4.3π/16) and again at 5.8π/16; 0-gap modes only for about 4.6–5.65π/16. "Below, neither side is topological" was wrong (a Floquet band's Chern number is a difference of gap windings). Window: about 4.5π/16 to 5.6π/16; outside it, extra light species at π.
- *B2e:* one draw per L, draws not nested; the x-momentum crossings fell 15–70× from L = 8 to 16 (unregistered reading; F1 tested only the largest gap); the control is uniform in x; only momentum transfers 2π/32 and 4π/32 probed. The eigensolver matches full diagonalisation to 4×10⁻¹⁵.
- *b2f_symmetry.py:* the row first labelled "mirror about each wall" is the one symmetric under s → L − 1 − s; label corrected.
- *Scope:* the speed excess s, the bound ℓ ≲ 3×10⁻³² m and the photon-decay figures are Wilson-walk figures; the massive wall mode's dispersion is not computed.

**Results docs.** Tuning 2's candidate removal entered README, STATE_OF_CO, ONE_PAGE, MATTER and TECHNICAL_APPENDIX with "shown in one space dimension, without interactions", the speeds unprotected, and the Wilson-walk scope.

**Ruled (on the correction above).** The inner-reflection ruling is withdrawn. The handednesses' speeds are not protected; tuning 1 takes a speed counter-term per handedness.

## 2026-11-14 — B2g: the cold read (reading)

**The −0.014 m was a fit artefact; corrected: no linear excess.** The registered fit (Δv = s − A/k² on k ∈ [5m, 20m]) returns s = −4.73×10⁻⁵ for every m when applied to an exactly relativistic E = √(m² + c₀²k²): the window scales with m, so the next relativistic term leaves a constant. B2g's values are that constant plus a term of order m². With measures free of this bias (D = E_m² − E_0² − m² on a fixed window; the speed residual against the walk's own relativistic form), fitting s = a m + b m²: at L = 16 the linear coefficient is |a| ≲ 0.003 across six coupling directions and row choices, tracking the leftover gap (a ≈ 2–3 m₀) and falling with L (L = 12: ~0.01; L = 20: ~0.0003); the m² term is a deficit in every case, its size set by the coupling (b from −1.2 to −9). The same measure on the 1D Wilson walk gives κ = 0.4157 = ½ sin w exactly (B2g's 0.405 was the fit's bias). Withdrawn: "about 25× smaller and negative"; "tops out just below light" is true of any massive particle and not a finding.

**Corrected reading.** In 1+1D, the domain-wall walk's massive mode carries no speed excess linear in its mass (zero up to the finite-L leftover); a deficit of order (mℓ)² remains, negligible for the electron and giving no vacuum-Cherenkov bound. If this carries to 3+1D, domain-wall matter has no electron Crab bound on ℓ from s.

**What 3+1D needs.** The wall must carry a 3+1D Dirac mode, which takes a 4-space-dimension bulk with a second-Chern-type phase. CO's 3D walk already has four components (α_j, β); a 4D bulk needs five mutually anticommuting 4×4 matrices (α₁–α₃, β and their product), which exist, so a four-component 4D walk is not ruled out, but it is not computed. On a 3+1D wall the mode is a two-spinor; a generic spin-turning wall-to-wall coupling n̂·σ is not a pure Dirac mass (E² = k² + Δ² ± 2Δ|k × n̂| for a model wall pair), so the coupling's spin form is part of the rule to be named. The 1+1D result does not carry over unchanged.

**Method.** A calibration passes only where the signal and the bias are of similar size; the bias is now also checked on an exact relativistic dispersion.

The reading is not tested where it matters (θ ≈ 10⁻²³, ln(1/θ) ≈ 53).

## 2026-11-14 — Induced light: I1's cold read, and small masses (I2)

**I1 corrections (cold read).**
- *Checked and holding:* filling angle < 0 equals the true E < 0 sea exactly (a determinant identity: Σ E = −arg det U stays 0 while no level crosses π); the energy signs (C_E = −Z_E q̂²/4, C_B = +Z_B q̂²/4); the units (c₀ measured 1.11118 places per step); the potential's placement (D U D, D²U, U D² isospectral) and its gauge consistency. "Both Z negative" is a real result.
- *Mechanism corrected:* the wrong sign comes from the three (π,0,0)-type corner species, whose eigenphase ±(5π/4 − θ) sits |π/4 − θ| from π (0.485 at θ = 0.3, 0.285 at θ = 0.5): at θ = 0.5 they are closer to π than the light species is to 0. A filled–empty pair more than π apart contributes with the opposite sign (δE_n = (a²/2) Σ_m |V_mn|² cot((E_n − E_m)/2)). Withdrawn: "heavy extra species"; "consistent with a positive log eating a negative lattice term" (the fall of |Z| follows the π-gap opening, a slope about 7× the log).
- *θ = 0.8:* past the π-gap closing at θ = π/4 (gap 0.015, smaller than the field), so the method fails there; its Z is dropped from any trend.
- *θ = 0.3:* a constant-A_y grid offset (about 8% of C_B at n = 1) moves c_ind/c₀ − 1 to −10% to −12%. I1's summary: induced light 10–18% slow at θ = 0.3–0.5.

**I2 (small masses; MATTER_runs/I1_induced_light, REGISTRATION_I2.md).** Exact second-order response on momentum pairs (validated against I1 to 10⁻⁷ in C_E), nested-cube Brillouin-zone quadrature.
- As registered, F1 and F2 failed through Z_B, which scaled as 1/θ²: the quadrature (not translation-invariant in k_y) left a finite response to a uniform A_y, which gauge invariance sets to zero, and the registered extrapolation had no constant term. Fix chosen before rerunning Z_B: fit C₀ + c₂q̂² + c₄q̂⁴ at q = θ/20, θ/10, θ/5, gated on C₀ matching the directly computed uniform-A_y response on the same quadrature within 2%. Gate: met to 10⁻⁴ for θ ≤ 0.1; missed at θ = 0.3 (3%; C₀ ≈ 10⁻⁹ there), outside the slope fit.

| θ | Z_E | Z_B | c_ind/c₀ − 1 |
|---|---|---|---|
| 10⁻³ | +0.0850 | +0.1015 | −1.6% |
| 3×10⁻³ | +0.0664 | +0.0803 | −1.0% |
| 10⁻² | +0.0474 | +0.0565 | −1.7% |
| 3×10⁻² | +0.0286 | +0.0332 | −3.1% |
| 0.1 | +0.0042 | +0.0036 | −16.9% |
| 0.3 | −0.0356 | −0.0352 | −10.5% (gate missed) |

- F1 (both positive at θ = 10⁻³): PASS. F2 (slopes within 10% of the leading log): PASS, dZ_E/d ln(1/θ) = 0.01648 (leading log 1/(6π²c₀) = 0.01520, +8.4%), dZ_B/d ln(1/θ) = 0.02006 (c₀/(6π²) = 0.01876, +6.9%). Intercepts −0.029, −0.037. The sign turns right between θ = 0.3 and 0.1.

**Reading.** Below θ ≈ 0.1 the induced field has the right sign and its growth is the light species' leading log within 7–8%; induced light runs within 1–3% of c₀ at θ ≤ 0.03. At physical masses (ln(1/θ) ≈ 53) the log dominates the finite parts: with leading-log slopes and the fitted intercepts, induced light sits about 4×10⁻⁴ below c₀ (with the fitted slopes, 8×10⁻³; the slopes' own 7–8% excess is not resolved). Either way the induced speed differs from matter's at 10⁻⁴–10⁻², far above the 10⁻²⁰ the data require: induced light does not remove light's speed counter-term. A single unit-charge species gives 1/α = 4π Z ≈ 11 at ln(1/θ) = 53; all known charged species, about 77 (tuning map). Light's bending c₂ (frequency-dependent terms) is not computed; the tuning-3 reading's main test is still ahead.

## 2026-11-14 — I2: the cold read (reading)

**Round-off in Z_E at the smallest θ, corrected.** Filled–filled pairs in different blocks (the same band at k and k + q, separated by about v·q) gave terms of order 1/(vq) that cancel exactly but not in floating point, leaving a q-independent offset that the constant-free extrapolation amplified (at θ = 10⁻³: 9- and 12-cell grids disagreed by 12%; the registration's grid check was at θ = 10⁻², where the effect is 100× smaller). Fix: drop filled–filled pairs (exact by unitarity; the second-order term reduces to (|X_nm|²/2)cot((E_n − E_m)/2), antisymmetric). Rerun with a free constant as a check (i2c_out.txt): Z_E = 0.08357, 0.06658, 0.04740, 0.02864 at θ = 10⁻³, 3×10⁻³, 10⁻², 3×10⁻² (constant term 10⁻⁶ of the signal). Z_B is unaffected (its fit's free constant absorbed the offset; rerun agrees to 0.1%). The pair method's formula, halving and counting were checked and hold; the Z_B fix reproduces every registered failed value algebraically (Z_B(registered) = 4c₂ + 5C₀/q̂₀²) and is an error fix, not a rescue. F1 and F2 still pass.

**Corrected reading.**
- *Slopes:* +6.1% (E) and +6.9% (B) above the leading log with a straight fit; the local slopes fall monotonically toward it (E: 1.124, 1.048, 1.018); with an O(θ) term fitted (allowed: the Wilson mass breaks θ → −θ), +0.8% and +1.6%. The leading log is reproduced to about 2%.
- *Induced speed:* c_ind/c₀ − 1 = −0.8%, −1.2%, −1.7%, −3.1% at θ = 10⁻³ … 3×10⁻², monotone. Universality fixes the slopes' ratio at c₀², so c_ind/c₀ − 1 ≈ Δ/(2 ln(1/θ)) with Δ the finite-part mismatch; from the data Δ ≈ −0.10, so c_ind ≈ c₀(1 − 10⁻³) at ln(1/θ) = 53. Withdrawn: "4×10⁻⁴ … 8×10⁻³" (it mixed slopes and intercepts from different fits).
- *The counter-term argument, restated:* the gap is suppressed only by the log, so 10⁻²⁰ would need Δ ≲ 10⁻¹⁸; nothing on the lattice forces the finite parts of Z_E and Z_B to match, and with several species (all at c₀) the leading logs stay at c₀ while the mismatches add, weighted by charge², with no reason to cancel.
- *Coupling:* 1/α = 4π√(Z_E Z_B) (unit charge, Heaviside–Lorentz, ħ = 1): about 11 for one species at ln(1/θ) = 53. The tuning map's 77 counts charged fermions only with a Planck cutoff; the W boson contributes with the opposite sign (roughly −44).

**Found while preparing the bending test (exploratory, not registered).** The static potential's response depends on the axis it varies along: at θ = 0.03, Z_E = +0.029 (x), +0.034 (y), −0.049 (z); the time-varying transverse response along y matches the y value. A brute-force real-space check matches the pair method to 10⁻⁷ along x and z (θ = 0.3). The palindrome's fixed nesting order (x outermost, z innermost) breaks cubic symmetry in the vacuum's lattice-scale (finite) parts while the leading log stays isotropic. For registration next: whether the spread stays finite as the log grows.

## 2026-11-14 — Induced light depends on direction (I4)

**Result (MATTER_runs/I1_induced_light, REGISTRATION_I4.md, i4_out.txt).** Registered outcome: anisotropic (spread of D_ij at θ = 10⁻³ is 36, against the 0.01 bar; the D metric is inflated where ε_z is small, and z-polarised speeds are undefined at θ ≥ 3×10⁻³ where ε_z < 0). In plainer form, the direction dependence is a constant offset in the finite parts, with the leading log the same in every direction:
- Permittivity: ε_z − ε_x = −0.0757, −0.0759, −0.0764, −0.0778 and ε_y − ε_x = +0.0065, +0.0064, +0.0061, +0.0053 at θ = 10⁻³, 3×10⁻³, 10⁻², 3×10⁻².
- Magnetic coefficient: for B along y (phases on x-hops varying along z, or on z-hops varying along x) about 0.063 below the other two (0.0384 against 0.1014 at θ = 10⁻³; offsets −0.0630, −0.0631, −0.0635, −0.0646); B along x and z agree to 3×10⁻⁴.

**Reading.** The palindrome's fixed nesting order (x outermost, z innermost) breaks cubic symmetry in the vacuum's lattice-scale response by an amount that does not shrink as the mass falls. At ln(1/θ) = 53, where the isotropic log parts are about 0.81 (ε) and 0.99 (Z_B), the offsets are 9% and 6%: induced light would depend on direction and polarisation at the level of several percent, against bounds many orders tighter. Induced from this walk, light needs direction-dependent counter-terms (more tuning), unless the walk restores cubic symmetry.

## 2026-11-15 — I4: the cold read; cycling the hop order (scout)

**I4 cold read.** The result holds: an independent real-space construction matches the pair method to 10⁻¹¹ for ε_x, ε_z, Z_B(x,z), Z_B(z,x), Z_B(x,y); Z_B(i,j) = Z_B(j,i) to five digits (the energy depends only on B). Corrections and additions:
- *The cause, exactly:* permuting the axes is identical to permuting the nesting order (spectra to 10⁻¹⁵, responses to 10⁻¹¹). The innermost axis carries the ε offset; B along the middle axis (built from the outermost and innermost hops) carries the Z_B offset.
- *The free walk is cubic where it matters at low energy:* identical along each axis, identical doubler quasienergies within each corner class (±2.386, ±1.541, ±0.815), non-cubic terms for the light species below 2×10⁻⁷ at |k| = 0.3; at generic zone points the spectrum differs under axis permutation by up to 0.35 rad. The anisotropy is lattice-scale (a loop effect), not inherited from the light species' dispersion.
- *Offsets, precisely:* ε_z − ε_x = −0.0757 − 0.071θ, B offset (against B along z) = −0.0629 − 0.057θ; a ln θ term is below 4×10⁻⁵. B along x and z agree to 3×10⁻⁴ for θ ≤ 10⁻² (1.2×10⁻³ at 3×10⁻²).
- *Speeds at ln(1/θ) = 53:* propagation/polarisation pairs range from −3.2% (along z, polarised x) to +5.0% (along y, polarised z); birefringence 2–5% per direction.
- *The registered metric* (spread of D_ij) was ill-posed (inflated where ε_z is small; undefined pairs dropped differently per θ); the offsets measure was added after the run and is the decisive form.
- *Other loop corrections:* tuning 1 would likely need its speed counter-terms per direction as well as per handedness unless the walk is made cubic: expected, not computed.

**Cycling the hop order (scout, i5_scout.py, i5_gap.py).** A walk that changes its nesting order from step to step (all six orders, or the three cyclic ones) has no gap: the multi-step unitary's quasienergies reach 0 and π (to 10⁻¹² at points found by minimisation), against the single walk's gap θ at k = 0 and 0.755 at π. The period-3 or period-6 cycle folds the walk's bands (single-step quasienergies up to 2.38) past the cycle frequency, creating new zero-quasienergy points: extra massless species and no vacuum. Excluded as a way to restore cubic symmetry (follows from the folding; no registration needed). Random orders per step are a stronger drive and inherit the problem. Remaining routes: a single-step walk cubic by construction (e.g. a body-centred-cubic Dirac automaton, Bisio–D'Ariano–Perinotti), or direction-dependent counter-terms.

## 2026-11-15 — The body-centred-cubic automaton as a cubic walk (C1, desk)

The Bisio–D'Ariano–Perinotti Dirac automaton on the body-centred-cubic lattice, checked in CO's terms (MATTER_runs/C1_bcc_automaton/C1_desk.md): unitary; doubled (a second light Weyl species at one class of three-fold zone vertices, and massless points at quasienergy π at the other class and the four-fold vertices); covariant only under binary rotations about the axes, so not cubic in its states (its dispersion is permutation-invariant, its spin directions are not); and its dispersion carries a direction-dependent term first order in kℓ (ω(k) − ω(−k) = −0.128 k² along a body diagonal), a dimension-five, helicity-dependent Lorentz violation that Crab synchrotron bounds exclude for electrons at any grain near the Planck length. Excluded as the route to cubic symmetry. Remaining: direction-dependent counter-terms on CO's walk, or a cubic single-step construction not yet known (silence).

## 2026-11-15 — Tuning 4 into the results docs: the cold read

The fourth tuning (light's electric and magnetic terms by direction) entered README, STATE_OF_CO, ONE_PAGE, TECHNICAL_APPENDIX and the tuning map after a cold read. Its corrections: the size is 0.8% (ε) and 0.5% (Z_B) per unit-charge species against light's own coefficients 1/(e²c₀) and c₀/e², and about eight times more summed over the known charges (Σ N_c q² = 8), not 0.7% in total; the counter-terms must hold to about 1 part in 10³⁰ (dimension-four birefringence bounds, Kostelecký and Mewes 2001), CO's sharpest tuning; four counter-terms if ε and 1/μ stay diagonal (off-diagonal entries not measured; up to ten); graded needed at w = 5π/16 only; "fixed nesting order" replaced by "the palindrome's nesting order" (README already excludes a fixed sequential hop order); "no cubic single-step walk is known" softened to silence, with two candidates to check (a sublattice-dependent order; Gupta–Short doubler-free walks in 3D). The same fraction holds whether light is posited or induced; for induced light a counter-term means light is no longer purely induced. Gauge self-consistency enters only at order e⁴ and cannot cancel it.

The measure, not the fall, fails inside 0.6r_s; a rerun with an absorbing centre (or the median as the registered measure) is needed before inside-the-horizon claims.

## 2026-11-15 — Inside the horizon on the flow step (F1f, F1g)

**F1f (registered).** Absorbing centre, median of the probability outside it: on the path at 2r_s and r_s (+0.04%, +0.03%), +2.03% at 0.5r_s and +5.5% at 0.3r_s: FAIL. Cause: taking the median after the leading part is absorbed (12%, 29%) biases it toward the back.

**F1g (registered after F1f, before running).** Each quantile carried along its own falling path, absorbed probability counted at the centre. At median 0.5r_s: quartiles −0.24%, +0.63%, +1.67% of their distance fallen. At median 0.3r_s (|b| ≈ 1.8): first quartile already absorbed (as predicted), median +1.14%, third quartile +2.25%: FAIL as registered, by 0.25% at the trailing quartile. Not re-registered.

**Reading.** On the flow step a pattern at rest in the falling frame crosses the horizon on the falling path (within 0.4%) and keeps to it inside, lagging by 1–2% by 0.3r_s, growing with depth and largest at the trailing side. A likely cause, not tested: tidal stretching inside the horizon pushes the packet toward lattice momenta (mean |k| reached 0.2 by 0.5r_s in F1e), where the shift carries at b·cos k, below b; a lag of order ⟨k²⟩/2 ≈ 2%. Grade for the docs: horizon crossing carried (F1e); the interior expected, with a lattice lag of a few percent deep inside, not shown to the 2% bar.

## 2026-11-15 — Inside the horizon: the lag explained (diagnostic, after F1g)

- *The lattice-carrying reading is withdrawn.* Measured ⟨1 − cos k⟩ stays below 0.002 to 0.3r_s; the integrated shortfall b(1 − ⟨cos k⟩) predicts 0.2 places of lag against 12 measured (f1h_lag_out.txt). The lag does not change with packet width (30, 60, 120: 13, 12, 15 places) but changes strongly with the absorber (a gentler, wider one gives 63 places): an artefact of the measure, not of the fall.
- *The cause:* F1g's distribution counted absorbed probability at the centre but dropped probability still sitting inside the absorbing region (masked, not yet absorbed), shifting every quantile outward.
- *Recount with that probability also placed at the centre (f1g_diag_inner.py, after the fact):* the median is on its falling path to +0.03% at 0.5r_s and at 0.3r_s; the quartiles are −1.1% and +0.9–1.1%, symmetric about it (the packet's own spreading, which carried quantiles ignore). A purely classical carry of the initial distribution through the same b reproduces the path exactly.
- *Grade for the docs:* horizon crossing carried (F1e, registered); interior on the falling path in a post-run recount (registered checks F1e–F1g failed through their measures). Expected, with the median on the path to 0.03% (diagnostic).
